According to a dis patah from Washington, President
Roosevelt yesterday reported to Congress the state of the lend-lease
transactions for the first half of this fiscal year. According to
it, the nation receiving the greatest benefit from the lend-lease
act was England. Ever since the outbreak of the war, she has re-
ceived 4,400,000,000 worth of war material: from America. He, also,
reported on America's aid to Chungking, but refused to make public
the figures for certain reasons.
The legislative body of Argentine, yesterday, adopted
a resolution to rcquest the government to expel the German ambassador
to Argentine from Argentine.
According to an AP dispatch from Cairo yesterday, the
allied mechanized troops of Italy and Germany invaded Egyptian
territory yesterday and clashed against the British troops.
According to a dispatch reaching Rome, the natives
along the border of Iran and Iraq reportedly have started a rebellion
and have already captured Hamadan, Kachia, Buristan and other places
(Names of places all phonetic). The reason for their rebellion is
believed to be anti-British.
The Nanking government today announced the appointments
of ministers to Spain and Rumania.
According to a report reaching Nanking, England,
America and Russia reportedly has rejected Chungking's request for
representation at the Moscow conference, which will be held shortly.
According to a report reaching Nanking, land slides
have occurred at about two hundred places along the Burma route in
Yunnan Province, thus completely stonping the flow of traffic to
Chungking.
Comment on current cvents:
Topic "The present condition of Manchoukuo":
The talk was of the usual lwngth and pertained to the progress made
by Manchoukuo. It mentioned how Manchoukuo was formed and the part
played by Japan in its formation. It termed the Manchurian incident
as the first step toward the destruction of the European and American
capitalistic oppression and the creation of a new order in East Asia.
It ended with the statement that the progress of Manchoukuo is an
evidence of what Japan's real motive in East Asia is.

tist: The talk was of alout ten minutes and pertained to the
hational services being rendered today by the women, youn and old,

she tried to picture the condition in Japan through the kind of
kimonos worn by thc women of Japan, today.

Aion JZK Program
te 9/28/41
Frequency 15,160 Kilocycles
News:
Japanese troops, operating in Hunan, finally captured
Changsha yesterday. Today, furious mopping up operations are being
carried on in and around Chengsha. In a statement pertaining to this
operation, Chief of Staff Ishiroku, who returned to Nenking today,
stated that the army is determined to carry on its activities as long
as Chiang Kai-shek does not reconsider.
In commemoration of the first annivcrsery of the tri-
partite pact, Japanese Ambassador to Germany Oshima and Japanese
Ambassador to Italy Horikiri both made a five minutes broadcast from
Berlin and Rome, respectively. Explaining the noble ideal 3f the
tripartite alliance nd describing the division of the world into the
status quo bloc and the new order bloc, Ambassador Oshima emphasized
the peace loving characteristic of the Japanese and ccncluded his
talk with the stetement that Jepan, firmly believing that real peace
is nl. poreible tir uh th.  tiblishe.cnt of . no. order, is
determined to accomplish her aim. On the other hand, Ambassador
Horikiri stated that Japan, Italy and Germany, today, are acting in
concert in the East and the West through the tripartite agreement and
explsined that in spite of the numerous obstacles lying in the path
of the tripartite alliance, Japan, Italy and Germany will overcome
these obstacles through their friendly feeling and mutuel confidence.
Okinori Kaya, president of the North China Development
Company, arrived in Moji vesterday and stated that the production of
important products is going along smoothly in North China, besides
emphasizing the necessity for the development of the mining industry
and the perfection of the transportation facilities.
A Japanese vessel, carrying 420 Japanese evacuees from
the Dutch East Indies, arrived in Kobe this morning. In regard to the
condition in the Dutch East Indies, they stated as follows: There are
still about 4, 500 Japanese in the principal places of Dutch East
Indies, but the Dutch East Indies authorities are doing everything
possible to compel these Japanese to return to Japan. Under such
conditions, it has become absolutely impossible for Japanese to
remain in business in the Dutch East Indies, for Japanese are being
excluded from ell sorts of business. Hence, the Dutch East Indies
has become the hardest place for the Japanose to live in the world.
The largest military operation in tho history of the
world has been concluded by the German troops operating in the region
east of Kiev. The number of Russiens captured in this operation was
665,o00, while the number of Russians wounded is estimated to be about
605,000. As a result, the numbcr of Russians captured since the
beginning of the war has risen to 2, 500,000, bringing the total number
of losses, including the wounded, to over 5,000,000.
According to a dispatch from Berlin, a spokesman of
the German government stated that German operations against the
Russians will be continued throughout the Winter.
-1-

=== Page 4 === (confidence 0.99)
According to a dispatch from Washington, Secretary of
Navy Knox, for the first time, has made known America's three
efense zones in an article published in the Department of Commerce's
Foreign Commerce Weekly. The first of these is the North Atlantic
route to Europe, the second is the southern route to both the Atlantic
and the Pacific coast of South America and the third is the Pan-
Pacific route to Australia, the Dutch East Indies and India. At the
same time, he emphasized the necessity for the nullification of the
neutrality law.
The ineffectiveness of the three rowers conference is
being reported in various forms by the reports from Moscow, London
and New York. According to the report from Moscow, En land and Amer-
ica has no intention to really aid Soviet Russia, but only wants
Russia to continue her fight against Germany to delay Germany's
attack against Britain. On the othor hand, reports from England and
America claim that Russia is making it difficuit for England and
America to aid her because of her refusal to furnish informetions in
regard to her domestic conditions.
Electrical transcription of Director of Information
Bureau Ito's broadcast ofer JOAK on September 26 in regard to the
tripartite agreement: (Electrical transcription very poor.) In
this talk, Director Ito (?) emphasized Japan's unshakable determina-
tion in regard to the tripartite alliance. He explained the signi-
ficance of this alliance as preventing the outbreak of world confu-
sion and establishing an era of world peace. Ho emphatically stated
that no matter how the situation may change, Japan's Axis diplomacy
will never be altered, and that Japan will fight against the attempts
of the third powers to estrange her from this Axis to the very end.
Japanese music.

News:
The Japanese troops operating in Hunan today captured
Shoshu (Hengchow?), an important railroad terminal. Besides, the
Japanese air force raided Koryo, south of Changsha, and caused consi-
derable damages to the military establishments and facilities there.
On the other hand, the Japanese naval air force yesterday raided
Shokan and Shokai in Canton Province.
According to a communique issued today, the Japanese
air force vesterday raided Chungking, Ryozan, Shiko and Onshi.
In return forthe luncheon sponsored by Foreign Minister
Toyoda yesterday in commemoration of the first anniversary of the
tripartite alliance, tho Italian and German embassies jointly spon-
sored a luncheon today at the Impcrial Hotel. About 3oo dignitaries
were present.
As a war preparation measure, the Ministry of Forestry
and Agriculture has completed the drafting of the regulations to in-
crease the production of agricultural and marine products. The
regulations will be presented to the cabinet shortly for approval.
Following the completion of the German troops' campaign
in the region east of Kiev, it was announced that German troops imme-
diately undertook a new movd. This move is believed to be a pincer
attach on Moscow. Hence, it is believed that a decisive blow will be
struck against Russia by Germany within the coming month.
According to a dispatch from Moscow, the representa-
tives to the three powers conference in Moscow arrived in Moscow on
September 28 and immediately called on Stalin. Hence, the three
powers conference can be thought to have actually started.
According to an announcement made by the British Air
Ministry, a great air duel took place on September 27 over the German
occupied territory in France. In this air duel, the British planes
reportedly shot down 24 German planes, while losing only 13.
According to a communique issued by the German High
Command, yesterday, German submarines during the past several days sank
12 merchant ships, totalling 67,000 tons, heading from Gibralter to
England. On the other hand, 12,00o0 ton tanker reportedly was sunk in
the waters of Southern Atlantic.
According to & di spatch from Washington, Sen. La Fol-
lette voiced a strong opinion against any change to be made in the
neutrality law, but President Roosevelt reportedly will confer with
party leaders today to draw up a bill nullifying the neutrality.
News of the past week:
News of the capture of Changsha on September 28 and
the achievements of the Japanese troops.

Comment on current eventy:
Topic "The Intornational Situation": (Condensed)
Japan, end Germany and Italy are like the two whecls of a car. If one
should break down, the other inevitably is affected. In this respect,
the situation in Europe affects Asia and vice-versa, and anything that
may seem to have nothing to do with Japan actually has a deep connec-
tion. This line of thought is applicable to the Moscow conference,
which was held from September 29 to October 1. Although the result of
the conference has not been made imown, it seems as if En lnnd and
Zmerica have promised to meet every demand of Soviet Russia in return
for the supplying of the neccssary raw materials to England and
America by Soviet Russia. In regard to its possibility of accomplish-
ment, it is believed to be impossible in view of the difference of
ideoloy, the lack of tr nsportation facilitatics and the condition
of England, herself. The reason Japan is greatly concerned of this
conference is because matters pertaining to Japan and the use of
Vladivostok as one of the ports of entry were undoubtedly discussed.
Thon, too, explaininy the futility of England and America's aid to
Soviet Russia, Chancellor Hitler, some tine ago, made a broadcast to
the effect that the war against Sovict Russia must be accomplished in
order to accomplish the war against England and thst Soviet Russia
mast be defeated because Bolshevism is an enemy of Nazism. Thus did
he refutc the runor of the possibility of an indcpendent peace between
Germany and Soviet Russia. Furthermore, he revealed the achievements
of the German troops since the beginning of the war, as follows: --
2,500,000 Russian troops captured, 22,000 guns taken, 18,000 tanks
captured and 14, 500 planes dostroyed. This broadcast of Hitler is
intercsting because the Hunan campaign, which has dealt a great blow
upon Chungking, was accomplished at about the same time. By these
facts, it is obvious asto which sido will cein the ultimate victory in
the east and the west. Hence, the attempt of England and Americe to
strengthen the ABCD encirclement is of greatest concern to our country,
because it is an attempt to hamper the accomplishment of the incident
and the creation of a mutual prosperity sphere in East Asia. A meet-
ing reportedly was held for a three day period, starting from October
3 and ending on October 5, at Manila between General McArthur (?),
commander of the merican troops in the Philippines, and Sir Brookes
Popham (?). commander of the British troops in East Asia. It was
also attended by representetives from Burma and ustralia and by the
-2-

=== Page 12 === (confidence 0.98)
n and British advisors to Chungking. The purpose of this con-
le can easily be seen, for it was nothing other than to streng-
the aid to Chungking and the BCD encirclement. What should
especially worthy of interest, at this time, is the fact that many
herican advisors, lately, have been sent to Chungking (Named some of
chem -- Lattimore, Clement, Hopes (?), etc.). No matter how they may
try, no power will be able to stop the creation of the East Asia
mutual prosperity sphere, for it is Japan 's highest ideal.

News:
In. spite of the sincere effort made by Japan ever since
Konoye's message to President Roosevelt on August 28 to peacefully
settle the Pacific problems, the anti-Japenese encirclement of South-
western Pacific has been strengthened all the more. Our country is
carrying out her national policy, based on the settlement of the in-
cident, the creation of a mutual prosperity sphere in East sia and
the observance of the tripartite lliance, for the preservation of
peace on the Pacific, and it is only proper that both imerica and
Japan should undertake their negotiation with a reciprocal spirit. If
America should be so obstinate as to make no attempt to understand our
noble ideal, the relation between Japan and America will inevitably
revert to the stage it was prior to Konoye's message and America
should wholly be responsible for it, thus stated the Nichi-Nichi Shim-
bun and the Yomiuri Shimbun of October 6. Bo that as it may, Japan
will calmly watch the developments with a firm determination and
attitude.
ccording to a dispatch from Hongkong, an Anglo-Ameri-
can-Sino economic conference has been started from October 6. The
purpose of the conference reportedly is to strengthen the economy of
Chungking through England and merica's aid. However, much expecta-
tion reportedly cannot be placed on the conference, in view of the
great difference in the posi tions of England, imerica and Chungking.
To Chungking, Shanghai is no longer of any use. To America, Shanghai
is of no great importamce to her because her small interests there.
As for England, she would not only lose her interest in Central China,
but throughout East Asia, were she to lose Shanghai. Therefore, it is
believed that it would be difficult for the three nations with such
differences to arrive at an agreement. Instead, it will result in
England folding up her flag and withdrawing fron East Asie.
America's military mission reportedly is expected to
arrive in Chungking shortly and according to  dispatch from Shanghai,
Chungking is making every preparation to please the mission. For
example, Kakutaiki, head of the foreign affairs office of Chungking,
issued the following statement on October 6: "We have no body to
depend upon but England and aerica and only through their aid can we
of Chungking act the advance-guards of the democracles."
According to a dispatch from Shanghai, the Chungking
government had planned to carry out its lutumn campaign some time
during the early part of this month, but has been foreed to postpone
the said campaign to next er in view of the Japanese troops drive
against Changsha and Teishu. . military conference to discuss this
compaign was also scheduled to be held, but has been postponed.
According to a dispatch from Hankow, Chiang Kai-shck,
greatly perturbed by the defeat of the Chinese troops in the Hunan
operation, has ordered the Chinese troops of t he 5th and 6th defense
area to recapture the strategic places in Hupeh Province. However,
-1-

=== Page 14 === (confidence 0.99)
Chinese troops are being repulsed with heavy losses by the
mese troops.
According to a dispatch from Berlin, the success of
the Japanese troops' operation against Changsha, Teishu and other stra-
tegic points of the Chinese troops has greatly impressed the military
experts of Germany. Even the newspapers of Berlin are commenting upon
the reports received from their reporters along the war fronts of
China.
According to a dispatch from Nanking, the Nanking
government has decided to establish a commercial affairs agency at
Haiphong and Saigon. This is worthy of note in view of the fact that
the freezing of Japanese asscts by England and America is necessi-
tating the creation of a en bloc.
According to a report reaching Taiyuan, the clash
between Chungking and the Chinese Red Army is becoming more serious,
of late. Chungking officals in Shensi Province reportedly have
formed a new army and is desperately trying to drive out the Chinese
Red Army from that area. On the other hand, the Chinese Red Army is
sending forth agents to destroy the new army fron the inside. Scores
of these agents reportedly have been captured and killed by the Chung-
king officials.
∴ccording to reports received from Berlin, London,
and other places, G rmany reportedly has concentrated about 3,000,000
troops along a 50o kilometer front sterting from the southern end of
Lake Ilmen to Bessrabu (phonetic) and has started a great drive
against Moscow. The German troons reportedly have already captured
Kalinin and Orel. Besides, according to an unofficial announcement
made by the German government, over 1,O00,O00 Russians have been cap-
tured in the campaign north of Kiev. Furthermore, the German troops
operating along the shore of the Sea of Azov reportedly have captured
Mariupol and Berdyansk.
According to an announcement made by the British
Finance Ministry on October 7, Britain's war expenditure for the first
four days of the latter half of this fiscal year is 65, 922, 891 pounds,
which is a new record. Hence, the total debt for this fiscal year to
date is 1,539,621,030 pounds.
Comment on current events:
Topic: "Significance of the three great Autumn cam-
paigns, which have shaken the foundation of Chungking": The talk was
nothing new, but the usual propaganda stating how these campaigns will
lead to the downfall of Chungking and how Chungking's source of suprly
has been cut off. It was merely a rehash of previous propaganda stat-
ing how determined the Japanese troops are in regard to the accomplish-
ment of the incident and how useless the aid of the third powers
toward Chungking is. The three campaigns mentioned were the Changsha
operation, the Teishu operation and the Canton operation.

months.

News:
Completing the selection of his cabinet members, Gen.
Tojo this afternoon presented his list to the emperor for his appro-
val, after which the investiture of the new ministers was conducted.
The new ministers are: Premier Hideki Tojo--Minister of war, home
and overseas. Shigenori Togo--Minister of foreign affairs. Admiral
Shigetaro Shimada--Minister of navy. Okinori Kaya--Minister of
finance.........Iwamura--Minister of justice. Kunihiko Hashida--
Minister of education. Lt. Gen. Chikahiko Koizumi--Welfare minister.
Hiroyasu Ino--Minister of agriculture and forestry. Vice-Admiral
Ken Terajima--Minister of communications and railways. Maj. Gen.
Teiichi Suzuki--Minister without portfolio. Shunsuke Kishi--Minister
of commerce and industry.
The newly formed cabinet is holding its first meeting at
five p.m. today at the premier's residence. After the meeting.
Premier Tojo is scheduled to hold an interview with press reporters
and talk to the nation over the JoiK station, in regard to the policy
of the new cabinet.
Personal history of Gen. Tojo--Native of Iwate prefec-
ture. 58 years old. Was chief of staff of Kwantung Army in 1937.
Appointed vice-minister of war in 1938. Was first director-general
of military aviation. Was war minister of the 2nd and 3rd Konoye
cabinets. Expected to carry out Japan's established policy.
Personal history of Shigenori Togo--Native of Kagoshima
prefecture. 60 years old. Entcred foreign office service upon
graduating from the Tokyo Imperial University. Stationed in China,
Manchoukuo, Europe and America. Was director of the Europe and
American Burcau and the Europe and Asia Bureau . Was ambassador to
Germany in 1937 and ambassador to Russia in 1938.
Personal history of Adm. Shigetaro Shimada. Native of
Tokyo. 59 years old. Graduate of Naval Staff College. Commanded
several warships. Was chief of staff of second fleet. Was principal
of naval school of submarine. Was chief of staff of third fieet.  Was
director of lst department of Naval Staff Board. Was commander-in-
chief of Japanese fleet in China waters. Was appointed commandant of
Yokosuka naval base on September 1 , this year.
Personal history of Shunsuke Kishi--Native of Yamaguchi
prefecture. 46 years old. Graduate of Tokyo Imperial University.
Entered service of Department of Commerce and Industry immediately
afterwards. Was transferred to Manchoukuo. Then, became vice-mini-
ster of cormerce and industry in 1940. Youngest member of the
cabinet.

=== Page 48 === (confidence 0.99)
Personal history of Vice-Admiral Ken Terajima--Native of
Wakayama prefecture. 60 years old. Graduate of naval academy and
staff college. Served as director of education and naval affairs
bureaus. Promoted to vice-admiral in 1932. Retired in 1934 to become
head of Uraga Dockyards, Dai Nippon Ordnance Mfg. Co.
In Italy, the reorganization of the cabinet is being re-
garded as a trend toward the strengthening of the Axis alliance to a
greater degree.
In Manchoukuo, it is being regarded as the greater
strengthening of the inseparable relations between Japam and Manchou-
kuo.
In Nanking, it is believed thnt the reorganization of the
cabinet will not have any effect upon Japan's established policy.
In America, it is bcing seriously regarded. For example,
President Roosevelt reportedly held a secret oonference with Secre-
tary of State Hull and other naval and military authorities Jesterday
to discuss the problems pertaining to Japan. It seems as if the Navy
Department is especially interested in the change of the Japanese
cabinet.
According to a dispatch from Shanghai, the formation of
the Tojo cabinet has caused considerable confusion within Chungking.
Comment on current events:
Topic "The resignation of Konoye's cabinet":
The record was not very clear to make possible a transaltion in detail.
It was mentioned that the cabinet was the shortest in the history of
Japan, but in its achievements it was second to none. In fact, the
commentator praised it as having successfully manifested its charac-
teristics as a war-time cabinet and began elucidating upon the various
domestic and foreign war preparation measures enforced by the cabinet.
He boasted of how the cabinet succeeded in enacting defensive measures
against the anti-Japanese encirclement and of the great progress made
in the creation of a new order in East Asia, both economically and
politically. He talked about the freezing act enacted by America and
England and how the said act is back-firing on them in East Asia. As
a conclusion, he mentioned how America and England are trying to
alienate Japan from the Axis powers and emphasized thst this change
of cabinet will in no way effect the established policies of Japan.

News:
The Tojo cabinet was completely organized yesterday
afternoon and following its first meeting, Premier Tojo, in en inter-
view with news reporters, made known the policy of the new cabinet.
He made a similar statement over JoAK station that evening. (An
electrical transcription of the speech followed, but was very bad).
In it, he stated that Japan's fixed policies--namely, the accomplish-
ment of the China incident and the creation of a new order in East
Asia--will be carried out and that the relations with the Axis powers
will be maintained. He also asked for the whole-hearted support of
the nation, stating that there is nothing that cannot be overcome with
determination.
Similar statements were made by Foreign Minister Togo
and Navy Minister Shimada.
The following appointments were also made:
Naoki Hoshino as secretary-general of the cabinet.
Masayuki Tani as chief of the information bureau
Eiichi Moriyama as director of the bureau of legislation.
Premier Tojo yesterday visited Ise Shrine to report his
appointment as premier and returned this afternoon by plane.  He
Immediately visited Yasukuni Shrine and Meiji Shrinc.
The nation is placing great confidence in the new
cabinet and is expecting the new cabinet to carry out the established
policies. Editorials of the Tokyo Nichi-Nichi and Asahi Shimbun were
read, but were indistinct.
In Germany, the formation of the new cabinet is being
looked upon favorably as an indication that Japan's relations with the

In Italy, the new cabinet is being regarded as a pro-
Axis one.
In Britain, political circles are showing signs of per-
turbation and will keenly watch Tojo's future policies.
In America, the government is not as perturbed as in

that will aggravate the relations between the two countries.

The talk, as indicated by the title, primarily concerned with the pro-

commentator termed, will eventually lead to the downfall of Britain,
because hmerica's aid, thus far, has been nothing but talk and will
continue to be so in view of Amorica's serious defccts. He summarized
the present situation as the "have not" nations creating a new order,
which is being opposed by the "have" nations.

Comment on current events:
Topic "The Tonth Anniversary of the Manchurian Inci-
dent": (Condensed) Ten years have passed since the outbreak of the
Manchurian incident and Manchoukuo has made a great economic, politi-
cal, and cultural progress within the ten years. The Manchurian in-
cident was brought about by the fact that conditions, caused by the
European and American nations, became so serious as to threaten Ja-
pan's interests in Manchoukuo, which she had fought for in the Russo-
German war. These conditions were brougit about by the Instigation
of anti-Japanism among the Chinese, the oppression of Japan through
such means as the Naval Disarmament Treaties, the League of Nations,
and their restriction of imports. Thus did the Manchurian incident
break out and it can be said that it was the first opposition to the
Versailles Treaty, which in turn led to ths awakening of Germany and
Italy. The following year Manchoukuo was formed through the wishes
of the people and recognized immediately by Japan. However, the
European and Anerican nations regarded this as a puppet government of
Japan and intensified their oppression against Japan. This led to
Japan's withdrawal from the Loague of Nations and the starting of her
venture of croating a now order in East Asia. Today, Manchoukuo has
developed into a great nation and is recognized by about a dozen
countries. This is solely duc to the fact that the principle of
-3-

=== Page 66 === (confidence 0.98)
Manchuukuo is to enable all people to live in contentment and peace.
Thus it can be said that this fact alone caused Italy and Germany to
awaken and fight for a new order in Europs. As for Japan, she
strengthened her effort for the overthrow of the influence of the
European and American nations and for the formation of a new order
in East Asia as the oppression and coercion of the European nations
became intense. Neverthelcss, the European and American nations
tried to disrupt peacc and regain their influence in East Asia by
inciting Chiing to fight against Japan, thus bringing about the cur-
rent incident. In spite of this, the Nanking government was formed
and this has resulted in the strengthening of the movement for the
creation of a greater East Asia mutual prosperity sphere. Hence,
Japan today is striving for the independence and self-sufficiency of
East Asia.

News:
Prince Takahito Mikasa was married to Princess Yuriko
Takagi this morning at the imperial palace.
According to a dispatch from Shanghai, Vice-Admiral
Muneichi Koga, commander-in-chief of the Japanese fleet in China
waters, today held his first interview with reporters on board the
Izumo and stated, as follows: I am thinking of doing my best toward
the accomplishment of this holy war. With the world situation becom-
ing more and more complicated every day, I keenly feel that the nation
should realize the necessity to fight this righteous war to the
finish at all cost.
Various hopeful observations are being made by
America, Britain, Chungking and the Dutch East Indies, or the so-
called nations comprising the anti-Jananese encirclement, who have
been more or less affected by the appearance of Tojo's nevw cabinet,
and even in regard to the Japanese-American negotiation, they seem
to think that it will be continued. However, our country's opinion in
regard to the Japanese-American negotiation will never be changed,
because of the existence of a definite point of view that must be
absolutely observed by our country. Although it vould be most de-
sirable if America were to further the negotiation with an understand-
ing of our country's sincerity in regard to the accomplishment of the
China incident and the actual situation in East Asia, the responsi-
bility for the termination of the negotiation must be placed upon
America, so long as she maintains her unreasonable attitude based on
misunderstanding and doubt. Therefore, if there is such a hopeful
observation that the Japanese-American negotiation will be concluded,
it must be said that it depends on the attitude of America. Thus,
our country is calmly watching the movements of America.
The troaty concluded among America, Britain, Soviet
Russia and Japan in l9ll for the protection of fur-seals will become
ineffective from October 22. At the time when this treaty was
signed, there were not more than 140,000 seals. Today, there are
more than 2,200,000 seals. As a result, they are causing consider-
able damages to the fishing industry. Hence, our country, last Octo-
ber, notified the signatory powers her desire to abrogate the treaty
one year hence, the final day falling on today.
According to a dispatch from Hanoi, the Japanese
delegation, headed by Masayuki Yokoyama, sent to make a survey of the
natural resources of French Indo-China, has been making preparations
ever since its arrival in Hanoi, on October 18 with the aid of the
government of French Indo-China. Yokoyama reportedly conferred with
Governor-General De Coux and other high officials today to discuss
the various problems connected with the making of the survey.
-1-

=== Page 82 === (confidence 0.99)
According to a report from French Indo-China, the
actual demarcation of the border between French Indo-China and Thai-
land was begun today. The work is expected to be completed by next
March.
According to a dispatch from Hanoi, the Japanese
culture exhibition was opened today in Hanoi. The opening ceremony
was attended by Governor-General Do Coux, Japanese Consul-General
Yukiyama (?) and other high ranking Japn ese and French officials.
According to a dispatch from Chungking reaching
Hongkong, a meeting was held in Chungking to discuss the currency
problem on October 19 between the representatives of Chungking and
those of Britain and America. Due to the difference of opinion, no
agreement reportedly was reached.
According to a communique issued by the German High
Command, the German troops reportedly have captured Dago, an island
strategically located at the mouth of the Gulf of Finland. Con-
sequently, Germany has now obtained completc control of the Gulf of
Finland.
According to a communique issued by the Russian
High Command, the Russian troops admittedly have been withdrawn from
Taganrog, thus threatening the fall of Rostov and enabling the Germans
to assume complete control of the Sea of Azov.
According to a dispatch from London, the opinion
that troops should be sent to continental Europe is being advocated
among the people. However, the government has issued a statement,
stating that the present rate of production of weapons by Britain
and America must be increased from three to four times before it
would be safe to land British troops on the continent to attach Ger-
many.
According to a dispatch from Washington, the
American-owned freighter Bold Venture, flying the Panamanian flag, was
torpedoed off Iceland on October 19. This was followed by the tor-
pedoing of the American flag ship Lehigh off the west coast of Africa
on October 21. Although the crew of the said ship made off safely
in three boats, two of the boats reportedly are missing. Both of
these announcements were made by the maritime commission. As a result
of these sinkings, the nation is wondering what the navy is doing to
prevent these sinkings, In view of the president's order to shoot
first.
According to a dispatch from Washington, President
Roosevelt, in an interview with press reporters on October 21, was
asked as to his plans in regard to America's aid to Russia, to which
he failed to make a definite reply. Although America should :send aid
to Russia in view of her promise made at the Moscow conference, it
appears as if America will make no attempt to send aid to Russia for
the time being, but watch the developments of the Russo-German war.

=== Page 83 === (confidence 0.99)
According to a dispatch from Lima, Peru, the Peruvian
Legislative body, yesterday, criticized the action of the American
government, which confiscated the airplenes destined for Peru some
time ago, and threatened to broak off relations depending on the expla-
nation of the American government. The logislative body also accused
America of trying to dominate the South American nations and mono-
polize the South American market.
Comment on current events:
Topic "The course to be taken by Japm":
Yesterday, the reassuring statement made by Foreign inister Togo on
the assumption of his office, and his statement to the offeat that
he will protect the rights of our country with a firm attitude,
accomplish the glorious historical mission of our country and contri-
bute toward world peace certainly must have been reassuring to all of
you, Japanese residing abroad. Welcoming the creation of Tojo's new
cabinet, the nation, today, is marching toward the accomplishment of
the difficulties together with Tojo's cabinet and is manifesting a
fiery determination to further the national prosperity with the
government and people uniting as one and watching for every opportu-
nity. Even the manner of living in the urban as well as in the rural
districts is something reassuring and courageous. This is because the
formation of Tojo's new cabinet has made the mental attitude of the
nations all the more strong and firm. In reporting the reactions of
the various nations, the newspapers of Japan are stating that thero
are not many examples in the history of Janan when the fornation of
a cabinet has drawn the attention of the world to such an extent as
the formation of Tojo's new cabinet. In regard to the foreign policy
of the new cabinet, especially, the nations of the world appear to
have various opinlons, nd I believe the newspapers and radios of the
places where you overseas Japanese are residing are making various
comments in regard to this. Hencc, the stronger the hostile feeling
of that place, where you overseas Japanese are residing, the creater
must be your anxiety in rogard to the course to be taken by Japan.
Today, I would like to center my talk on the strong dctermination ox-
pressed by Tojo's new cabinet, and explain the course to be taken by
our country, Japan. Firstly, let me repeat the first words uttered by
Premier Tojo. He stated, "Our country, today, is facing an unpre-
cedented crisis, but it need not be mentioned that this crisis is a
crisis for the creation of the East Asia mutual prosperity sphere by
our country, Japan, as a power of the world". Even Foreign Minister
Togo explained that instances of a nation making great progress with-
out confronting considerable difficulties are rare in the history of
the world and that Japan's future will be extremely promising if
this critical test is overcome, and this is very true. Hence, the
people of Japan are regarding these words of the foreign minister as
their own words and are doing their utmost in thcir respective line:iof
work for the prosperity of our country. Now, let us consider the
ultimate aim of our country that should be accomplished for the
furtherance of our national prosperity. In regard to this, Foreign
Minister Togo explained, "It is necdless to mention that our country's
ultimate aim is the preservation a nd promotion of world peaco, but
should it involve or even threaten our national security, we must pro-
tect it with a firm attitude and further the accomplishment of our
historical mission. Conscquently, our country's diplomacy, backed by

=== Page 84 === (confidence 0.99)
a strong connection with armament preparation, should be based on a
strong and righteous belief that is always focused on the realities,
thus furthering our national prosperity and contributing toward worid
peace", and concluded, "If this point of view is firmly adhered to and
this righteousness is furthered without fear and haste, it is clearer
than looking at a fire that this national crisis wi ll be overcome and
that our country will enjoy a rising fame". Thus did he reveal the
noble ideal sought by our country's diplomacy. Even in the statement
made to the nation by Premier Tojo as minister of war, this firm
determination upon which the new cabinet hns been created is revealed.
In it, he explained that it is of absolute necessity tint the esta-
blished policy for the accomplishmen t of the Incident be maintained
and that the nation and army unito solidly toward this aim in order
to gouge out the roots of the evils of this Incident and make our
country safe forever. Herein, too, is clearly revealed the course to
be taken by our country, Japan, who is striving to accomplish the
Incident, establish the East Asia mutual prosperity sphere, strengthen
her relations with the fri endly nations and thus contribute toward
world peace. In spite of this, some of the hostile nations are
making various speculations In regard to the formation of Tojo's now
cabinet. I believe thnt such malicious observations are being made
in the places where you are residing, but it can be rendily under-
stood that these nations, which are unjustly attompting to obstruct
Japan's national policy, will experience greater fear as Japan's
administrative powers are further strengthened and the aim of her
national policy becomes that ruch clearer. As for the hostile
nations, they may be attompting to chock our country's just progress
for their selfish motive, but none of then is able to, or is bold
enough to, manifest their un-called for challenge against our country
with their own strength. Due to this fact, they are making various
speculations and are being frightened by their own.......... This is
especially true of the Southern nations, which have been attracted to
America's influence, for in spite of the fact that they have lost
their independent nature and are now hardly able to walk by them-
selves, they are still uttering strong words. Nevertheless, these
nations, wilfully interpreting our diplomatic power, which, in all
likelihood, might be put into action under a completo unity between
the supreme command and the state administrators, as a new threat,
are in a state of great confusion. This great confusion of theirs,
it can safely be snid, is certainly the revelation of their evil ambi-
tion, which has made it impossible for them to uppreciate Japan's
intention. Even in regard to the Japanese-American negotiation, if
America awakens from her cvil dream of dancing to the tune of the
policies of Chiang Kai-shek and comes to appreciate the noble spirit
of Japan, who regards the China incident as a holy war and the crea-
tion of the Greater East Asia mutual prosperity sphere as conducive to
world peace, peace on the Pacific, which America desires, will cer-
tainly be maintained. In regard to this, America should be able to
understand reasonably if she takes into consideration her Monroe Doc-
trine, which she is adanantly emphasixing. Consequently, the most
urgent aim of Japan, at present, is the accomplishment of the China
incident, and she, pivoting on the Axis relations, is exerting her
wholo effort to the creation of a new order and a mutual prosperity
sphere in East Asia. Since the intention of Tojo's new cabinet is to
-4-

=== Page 85 === (confidence 0.99)
arry out this policy, it is needless to mention that the actions of
the third powers, who are attempting to obstruct this, will be
sternly challenged. Hence, I would like to close my talk today with
the statement made by Minister of Navy Shimada, which is, as follows:
"The imperial navy, while striving for the accomplishment of the
China incident on one hand, is making every preparation to enable her
to cope with the ever-changing situation and from the officers to
the men, they are all carrying out their duties under the august vir-
tues of the emperor. Although the situation, today, is very critical,
it is my belief that the nation can be made secure if the army and
navy and the government and her people cooperate and march toward the
accomplishment of our country's aim".

Comment on current events:
Topic "Special Envoy Kurusu's mission to Arcrica": (Record
was not very distinct, but as much as possible has bee: translated).
As a manifestation of her eagerness and effort to maintain the peace
of the Pacific and thus contribute toward world peace, our country has
decided to dispatch Special Envoy Kurusu to America to aid Ambassador
Nomura in the Japanese-American negotiations. The said envoy left
Tokyo on November 5(?) to assume his post in Washington. Before going
further let us consider the situation in East Asia. When the Tojo
Cabinet was formed, Britain and America, feeling very uncertain about
the future of the Pacific, became very perturbed, it seems. For ex-
ample, Secretary of the Navy Knox openly stated that the situation has
become such that a clash between Japan and America is inevitable. As
expected, the authorities in Washington were startled by this fiery
remark of Secretary Knox and explained that it was the personal opinion
of Secretary Knox. If it were truly the personal opinion of Secretary
Knox, it would not be objectionable, but America's Far Eastern policies
executed since clearly indicate that it is the unanimous view of the
Roosevelt regime. That is, her act of aiding Chiang Kai-shek to make
Chungking the advance post of her continental offense in the Pacific,
together with the activities of her military mission hended by Admiral
Magruder, has becomo all....the active....and even in regard to her
Far Eastern defense, not only has the air force of...........been en-
rolled into the American force, but the leaders have been changed and
even the reserves are being called to active duty. Hence, Amcrica is
busily perfecting her war preparation in such a manner, and if the ap-

break at the slightest touch. In regard to the attitude of our

right of her existence and seek for the understanding cooperation of

The primary reason why the consummation of the Japanese-Americun nego-

involving the Pacific. Again, the reason why America, today, is

she can make our country yield without fighting through an economic
blockade. Probably nothing is more nonsensical than this. Indeed,
it is absurd. Through the five years' ordeal of the holy war, the
strength of our land and sea forces has become all the more superior
and with the determination of the nation to overcome this crisis be-
coming all the stronger, a war preparation has been completed to cope
with any emergency. Nevertheless, inasmuch as the leaders of America
are making no attempt to......

News:
Something about the conclusion of the new campaign started
at a point north of Shinyo, Hunan Province. Annihilation of the

killed and many arms and munitions taken.
Report from the front on the great achievements of the
Japanese troops operating in the southern region of Shantung Province.
Chinese troops of the Chinese puppet government reported assisted
in this campaign.
The Nippon Kinyu Kyogi Kai (Japan Financial Federation)
held its board of directors meeting today at the Tokyo Bankers' As-
sembly Hall. Finance Minister Kaya, who attended this meeting on
the invitation extended by the said association, explained the
financial condition in Japan, urged the cooperat ion of financial
circles with the government's policy and stated that Britain and
America's freeze act, which had been enforced to disrupt the economy
of our country, instead has elevated our country's position and
driven out Britain and America's influence from East Asia.
It was announced today through the oficial government
gazette that the 78th session of the Diet will be convoked from
December 26.
Manchoukuo's development project is about to end its first
five year plan in March of next year. To datc, about 100,000 Japan-
ese have gone to Manchoukuo to take part in its development. With
plans to undertako its second five year plan next year, Manchoukuo
is expected to make greater progress and to achieve this aim, a de-
velopment farm law (kaitaku nojo ho) was passed on the 13th. The
law will become effective in March. By this new law, Japanese will
be allowed to possess land and to hand their estate down to their
decendents and contribute toward the development of Manchoukuo's
agricultural industry through the principle of mutual prosperity and
cooperation. Furthermore, in regard to the second five year plan,
it is being discussed among the various Japanese and Manchoukuoan
officials.

With the thickening of the dark clouds over the Pacific,
the press circles of Japan, focusing their attention upon this, are
commenting as follows in regard to the situation in America: (Asahi
Shimbun of the 19th) As a matter of fact, America is known as a
"have" nation, but in spite of the fact that she is well favored with
natural resources, it is doubtful as to whether she would be able
fully to utilize her natural resources in time of an emergency, for
she is experiencing a great shortage of labor. That is, although

or 1,500,000 are skilled workers and 40%, or 1,7000,000, are semi-
skilled workers. The rest are all unskilled. In addition to this,
American labor has not yet been organized on a war footing. In
fact, large-scale strikes arc breaking out now and then........
it can be said that America is lacking the all-important elements
for the carrying out of modern war.
According to a dispatch from Rome, an Italian paper today
commented upon the Far Eastern situation, as follows: "The Japanese
Government, at present, is exercising patience and caution and is
regarding the preservation of peace as her primary policy. However,

attitude taken by President Roosevelt against the Axis powers, to
say nothing of America's arming of merchant ships and trespassing
through the war zone. Hence, America is wholly responsible for the
crisis on the Atlantic and the Pacific."

According to another dispatch from Canton, in spite of the

According to a dispatch from Moscow, the German front

and are now intensifying their drive against Sebastopol.
According to a DNB dispatch, the German Air Force on the

of the south and also caused fire to break out in 39 supply trains.
In regard to the German troops' operation in Crimea, a

Odessa and Kherson are already in the hands of the Germans, the only
Russian bases left are Sebastopol and Novorossisk in the Black Sea.
If the former should fall, the Russian Fleet in the Black Sea will
become absolutely powerless.

land.

Comment on current events:
Our country is confronting an unprecendented crisis amidst
this complicated internationel situation. What our countrymen,
who alrendy are fortir ied with a formid ble determination to
overcone this crisis, have been most esrnestly desiring latcly is

of the nations surrounding Japon and its fundamental policy to
cope with the situstion. Hence, the message rendered by Premier
Tojo bofore the Diet on the 17th clearly and strongly explained
exactly what the people hnd been wanting to hear. Today, I would
like to talk of the significance of Premior Tojo snd Foreign
Minister Togo's speeches and the internal and external repercus-
sions causcd by the spueches. Premier Tojo and Foreign Minister
Togo's messages to the Diet clarified the exact state of the
aggravated situation surrounding our country and fully revesled
our country's earnest belier and determination to overcome
whatever situation that may arise out of the Japsnese-American
nogotiation, which is at the crossroads of success or feilure.

that Japan has been negotiating with America for the fundomental
solution of the Japanese-American problems since April and
explrined the three great diplomatic policies adhered to by our
country. Premier Tojo summarized it as follows: (1) That the

News of the Russo-German War:
Judging from reports from Berlin, Moscow, and other places,

roported that snow is so thick that they are blinding attempting to

Comment on Current Events:
In his message of tho 17th to the Dict, Premier Tojo
pointed out the strongthening of the attompt of Amcrica, Britain
and the Netherlands to disrupt the peace of East Asia, both eco-
nomically and militarily and emphasized that since such an act not
only hampers the settlement of the China Incident, but also affects
the existence of our country, it absolutely cannot be overlooked,
thus making clear our determination. In regard to the condition
in the southern part of the Mutual Prosperity Sphere, Major Tominaga
of the Army Information Bureau stated as follows: "Inasmuch as they
are carrying out acts threatening our very existence and hampering
the creation of a new order in East Asia, we absolutely cannot over-
look their acts." Thus did he strongly express the determination of
our army. Today, let us hear what Major Kanetaro Tominaga of the
Army Information Bureau said: (Electrical transcription of his
talk). "The nations which are opposed to the creation of a new or-
der in East Asia have been carrying out military and economic co-
ercion against our country ever since the outbreak of the incident.
With the landing of our troops in French Indo-China especially, the
hostile nations, composed of America, Britain, Chungking, and Dutch
East Indies began to manifest bluntly their sinister hands and
formed the so-called ABCD anti-Japanese encirclement. Taking con-
certed action, thus, both militarily and economically, they have
oppressed and threatened our country and have needlessly stirred up
the waves of the Southwestern Pacific, as you probably are awarc of
through the newspapers and radio. Now let us consider the degree
of their anti-Japanese military oppression, as to whether their arma-
ment is sufficient to oppress and threaten our country. I believe
that this should be considercd seriously. It is America (?) who is
actively trying to strengthen the anti-Japanese encirclement as
-3-

=== Page 136 === (confidence 0.99)
part of her defensc in the Southoastern Pacific. Malaya, Dutch
East Indies, India, Burma, and Chungking are nothing but puppets
who rely upon America, who has a formidable navy, and who have aim-
lessly formed the anti-Japanese encirclement. It must be said that
their attitude to remain complacent still, in spite of having lost
their self consciousness as nations of East Asia and of having
placed their fate in the bonds of America and Britain, is certainly
pitiful. Inasmuch as the anti-Japanese encirclement is such, we
should not be misled by the propaganda that we hear, but should.....
the sinister hands of America and Britain.
Hence, let us consider what the real strength of this
anti-Japanese encirclement is. Counting the troops of Malaya, Dutch
East Indies, Burma, Hawaii, Philippines, Hongkong, British Borneo
and India, it is estimated to be about 300,O0o. Besides this it
appears as if they have from 100 to 300 planes stationed at their
various air bases. In addition to this, when the troops under
Chiang Kai-Shek are included, the numerical strength of the anti-
Japanese encirclement amounts to over 300,000, besides close to, or
over, 1,O00 planes. However, this 1s merely an estimation. When
the armament of the various localitics is compared with that of the
previous world war, it can be said to have been increased two-fold
at least and 7 or 8-fold at places. In Malaya, for example, Britain
has stationed about 8 times more troops than the number stationed
there during the World War and with its main strength stationed
along the border of Thailand and Malaya, Britain is not only threat-
ening and obstructing Thailand's rapprochement toward Japan, but is
also cocrcing Thailand to join tho anti-Japanose encirclement. In
a word, several hundred thousand may sound liko a formidable mili-
tary strength, but since only a few are British, Australian, and
American troops and the greater part are native troops, not only is
their equipment and training very bad, but their spirit, military
discipline, and (effectiveness?) are, in a word, very poor and im-
perfect. Of course, their numcrical strength may amount to several
hundred thousand when counted as one, but since they are scattered
over a widc area, such as Burma, Yunan, and Dutch East Indics, they
cannot be cxpected to have the power of combination as that of the
army of a single nation.
It must be said that this fact is the grentest weakness
of the anti-Japanese encirclement, which Britain and America, who
are excellent propagandists, are desperately trying to strengthen
whenever possible. However, to say nothing of the quality of their
present numerical strength, it should be regarded as a matter of
grave concern that these nations of East Asia, misled by Britain
and America, are regarding our country as their enemy and are
strengthening their armaments on one hand and are conducting an
economic blockade to choke our country to death on the other hand.
Such acts, as to disregard their actual strength and cooperate in
-4-

=== Page 137 === (confidence 0.99)
the attempt to check our country, who is striving to liberate East
Asia, even at the risk of war, should be regarded with grave con-
cern. So long as they continuo to carry out acts threatening our
very existence and obstructing the creation of a New Order in East
Asia, it is only proper for our country who, enduring the unbearable,
has tolerated their challenging acts and economic oppressions and
maintained the peace of tho Pacific, to be absolutely unable to
overlook the situation. At the same time, it is ncedless to state
that the time will come when we will have to show that there is a
limit to our patience. So long as one end of thc ABCD anti-Japanese
encirclement, as I have mentioned above, is America we will have to
strengthen further and solidify further our dotermination. In this
respect, I sincerely hope that the nation will strive to assume a
determined attitudc."

77th emergency session of the Diet, which was actually convoked yes
terday. After the ceremony of the opening, the House of Commons
adopted a resolution thanking the officers and men fighting at
the front. A similar resolution was adopted by the House of Peers.
Several measures of an urgent nature were discussed in bot'! houses,
also. (Text of the resolution was read).
According to a dispatch from Washington, the great trans-
continental plane, carrying Special Envoy Kurusu, arrived in Washing-
ton at 7:30 o'clock last night giving him no opportunity to shake
hands with Ambassador Nomura, the newspaper reporters crowded him.
Later he left for the Japanese Embassy together with Ambassador
Nomura and had dinner there with the members of the Embassy. He is
expected to spend the 16th in conference with Ambassador Nomura and
start his negotiation from the 17th. In an interview with Japanese
newspapermen on the 15th, Envoy Kurusu stated that the American
Navy offered to furnish a Navy Plane to Honolulu (during the Midway
breakdown of the Clipper). He also stated that there is still room
for an understanding, though it is difficult. With the arrival of
Envoy Kurusu, the attention of the world has been cast toward the
scheduled meeting between President Roosevelt and Secretary of State
Hull, and Ambassador Nomura and Envoy Kurusu on the 17th. In regard
to America's attitude toward the coming of Envoy Kurusu it can be
mentioned that Pres. Roosevelt postponed his proposed trip. On the
other hand, she has shown an attitude to avoid the Japanese-American
negotiation. It is hoped that America will cast away her misunder-
standing and doubt and show some sincerity toward the preservation
of the peace of the Pacific. It is expected that Envoy Kurusu will
frankly relay the real sentiment of Japan. Hence, everything will
depend upon the attitude of America.
Special Envoy Yoshizawa, who is in French Indo-China
stated, "Whatever situation may arise, French Indo-China is prepared".
In connection with the Pacific problems, the position of French Indo-
China is drawing considerable attention, but, Envoy Yoshizawa
stated, "French Indo-China, where the interests of America, Britain,
Dutch East Indies and France are complicated, will be effected by
the Japanese-American negotiation, but no matter how the negotiation
develops, it is necessary for us to be prepared to meet whatever
situation that may arise and this preparation has al ready been com-
pleted".

Comment on Current Events:
Topic: "The Amendment of the Military Service Law":
Yesterday when the historic 77th emergency session of the Diet was
convoked amidst the aggravated international situation, the army
undertook the revision of the military service law and announced the
revision through the official government gazette as effective imme-
diately. The aim of the revision is to strengthen the National
defense footing. In other words, the law has been revised to make
possible the enlistment of those of class C, who were rejected in
the past, together with the shortening of the students' school term
and the shortening of their period of exemption from military service,
which were enacted s me time ago, this revision has literally made
possible the realization of the motto that all men of our country are
soldiers and can be said to be a historical one. In spite of our
country's noble policy to preserve and promote world peace, the inter-
national situation centering around our country is becoming aggra-
vated day by day. The fact that such a revision making possible
the military service of all our youths has come into being at such
a time should be regarded as a great step toward the strengthening
of Emperor Meiji great policy. Now, I would like to talk about the
way this new revision will function, upon what it will be focused and
its significance. Firstly, the deferred class, which formerly were
not inducted, will be conscripted in the same manner as the recruits.
-3-

=== Page 161 === (confidence 0.99)
residing in China, Hongkong and Amoy are being presently
residing in a foreign country and the reservists, who
ng in these areas, have been exempted from the annual
ervice. However, the new revision has done away with these
Tons. In other words, they will be treated in the same manner
nose residing in Manchoukuo and will be conscripted into mili-
ry service just as those resi ding in Japan proper. Again, those,
who have already served and were placed on the reserve list, will be
called at any time. Furthermore, those having volunteered for one
year service and placed on the reserve list and those serving now
will all be treated in the same manner as the graduaten of military
academy (?) Hence, Officers will be ....... up to 5l years of age.
The above mentioned is the gist of the revision. As you all know,
military service is divided into the lst militia and the 2nd militia.
The 2nd militia comprised of those deferred for weak health and being
unfit for military service. Even if they regained their health
later, they were not conscriptod into military service in the past.
However, ever since the strengthening of the national defense footing
became an urgent matter, the reoxamination of those deferred came to
be discussed. At the same time, the desire to serve at the front
among these deferred became stronger. Hence, it can be regarded
that the revision was made to comply with the wishes of the nation.
When this revision is considered in connection with those residing
abroad, a similar thing can be said. In other words, the expansion
and development of our National power has resulted in a greater number
of Japanese going abroad. Above all, the going of Japanese continen-
tal Asie has been tremendous since the creation of a new order in
East Asia Mutual Prosperity Sphere became our country's national
policy. Furthermore, more and more of these came to express their
desire to be treated in the same manner as those residing within
Japan proper and to serve in the army. And with the perfection of
our outside military agencies for the handling of the conscription
of those abroad today, it was finally decided to make the revision.
Therefore, it can be said th t the revision of the military service
regulation was born to meet such internal and external conditions.
In other words, this revision makes possiblc to everyone the porfor-
mance of his military duty and the establishment of an impregnable
national defense footing to cope with the international situation.
Hence, it can be said that the revision of the military service regu-
lation necessary for the perfcction of a strong national defense
footing has been completed through the enthusiasm of the people.
(Rocord indistinct) The spirit to progress together with the people,
expressed by Premier Tojo when the cabinet was formed, has penetrated
into the spirit of the people and has resulted in daily life of the
people being changed to conform with the policy of the government.
Even when the announcement was made that taxation would be increased
tho nation manifested their acceptance with the remark that it is
a duty to be performed in order to win a war and that they are will-
ing to risk everything for the preservation of the nation's fate and
honor. A similar spirit is also being manifested toward the revision
of the military service law. That is, everyone is prepared to sacri-
fice his life for his country, and it is hoped that all will enlist
with this spirit. Our country has gained invaluable experiences
through the four years of the Holy War and with these experiences
as a basis, she has extablished an unshakable footing, both economi-
cally and militarily, In other words, our country has now established
-4-

=== Page 162 === (confidence 0.99)
thstand whetever oppression may be inflicted upon her
nations. With the revision of the military service
ooting has been made firmer and more solid and unshakable.

News:
The Japanese troops operating in the southern part of the
Shantung Province have practically captured all of the enemy's
strategic positions and have completed an encirclement of the enemy.
The known successes up to yesterday are 813 killed, 134 captured,
and many arms and munitions taken.
Prior to the convocation of the Diet, the government has
decided to confer with leaders of the parliament and the press. This
afternoon, the first of the leaders to be called was ........On that
occasion, Premier Tojo stated, "Japan is now facing the cross-roads
of life and death. On the occasion of this crisis, I am determined
to accomplish our immovable policy, together with the people. The
reason why the government has called for the emergency session of the
Diet is to overcome this difficulty." In reply to this, Speaker
Koyama of the House of Representatives stated, "We would like to
cooperate with the government and contribute toward the prosperity
of our country."
To strengthen his array of advisors and be prepared for
the coming emergency session of the Dict, Foreign Minister Togo de-
cided to appoint Naotaka Sato, an ex-foreign minister, and Kawagoe,
former Ambassador to China, as his advisors.
With the approval of the Emperor, the cabinet today decided
to carry out the reorganization of the administrative agencies in
order to expedite business.
According to a dispatch from San Francisco, the Pan-Ameri-
can Airways today announced that Special Envoy Kurusu will arrive on
November i3, a day later than schedulod, due to engine trouble of
the China Ciipper at Midway. The repairing of the engine trouble is
being hurried, but if repairs are not completed by the scheduled
time, it is believed that another plane will be sent out to Midway
to take Envoy Kurusu across the Pacific to Mainland America. With
the arrival of Envoy Kurusu in Washington not far away, various
speculations are being made, but Ambassador Nomura, in a press inter-
view with reporters yesterday, stated, "I firmly believe that there
is a possibility to settle peacefully the differences between Japan
and America in regard to the problems of the Pacific. I am determined
to do my best to find the formula, by which Japan and America can
exist peacefully. If I, as ambassador, am able to restore the
friendly relations between Japan and America, it will not only be of
help to America and Japan, but to the world also."

=== Page 165 === (confidence 0.99)
....Tokutomi, a Nestor of the press circle of Japan, at-
tended the meeting to discuss the emergency, sponsored by the Nichi-
Nichi Shimbun, and gave a two-hour talk entitled "Looking at the
Pacific Situation". In regard to the course to be taken by Japan,
Tokutomi emphasized that Japan should cooperate with the Axis powers
and march toward their mutual aim, thus contributing toward world
peace. He stated that the Asiatics should not hesitate to do any-
thing in the Orient and that Chiang Kai-shek is the watch-dog of
President Roosevelt. He urged that Japan should seek for the recon-
sideration of America who is fluttering the dead nine-power pact be-
fore Japan's eyes. He claimcd that things have come to such a pass
because America is sticking her hands in somebody else's pie and
that 1f America would mind her business, the matter could be peace-
fully settled. He concluded that if the nations of the world would
do what is right, the world would be a happy place to live in. Thus
did he criticize America. He also stated that America maintained
friendly relations with Japan during the Meiji Era up till Japan's
victory in the Russo-Japanese war. Then, she became jealous, he
claimed, because she hated to see another country become strong.
He explained that America wants to be the greatest in the world
in everything and wants to check the nations that make any headway.
With the idea that the Pacific is her lake, America, Tokutomi stated,
is trying to check and scare Japan. No matter how America may try
to check Japan, he asserted, Japan will become a great nation for
she is taking a course that should be taken. Thus did he talk in a
fiery tone for about two hours.
According to a dispatch from Nanking, Ambassador to China
Honda today left for Tokyo by plane. He is expected to confer with

his jurisdiation. During his absence, Minister Hidaki (?) will carry
on the business affairs. Prior to his departure, Ambassador Honda

but our national policy in regard to the accomplishment of the inci-
dent will nover be changed. Besides, since the various measures
aiming for the strengthoning of the Nanking government are already
under way, there need by no anxiety. I am hoping for a short vaca-
tion in Japan."
The new Chinese ambassador to Japan arrived this morning

less to mention, I will do my best in thc execution of my new dutics.
(Record interrupted by static). I believe that I will be able to

According to a dispatch from Washington, about 3,500 AFL

Comment on current events:
Topic "This week's review of the international situation":
In spite of our country's desperato effort to promote and preserve
the peace of the Orient as well as that of the world, the problems
in the Pacific are becoming very critical due to the unreasonable
attitude of America. On the other hand, German troops are making
steady progress in Europe, in spite of Britain and America's aid
to Soviet Russia. Thus the movements of the world are pivoting
around these problems. First, let us consider this movement in the
light of Far Eastern problems. The Japanese government has dis-
patched Special Envoy Kurusu to America to aid Ambassador Nomura in
Japanese-American negotiations. On the 15th of this month, Special
Envey Kurusu left Tokyo and is presently hurrying towards Washington
What I would like to say in regard to this is that the dispatching
of Special Envoy Kurusu to America is the outward manifestation of
our country's noble desire to settlo peacefully, if possible, the
-4-

=== Page 168 === (confidence 0.99)
Pacific problems which have become very critical. Not only does
this fact not mean that Japan has bowed to America, but it also
does not mean that Japan is afraid of America's strength. Japan,
in other words, is undertaking the last (?) diplomatic negotiation
to sette peacefully, if possible, the problems upon which the fate
of the Pacific depends, through America's reconsideration at the
last five minutes. As for the Japanese people, there is none with
an optimistic view in regard to Special Envoy Kurusu's mission to
America. This is because when the n: ws that Special Envoy Kurusu
would be sent to America was received in Washington, Secretary of
State Hull stated that the dispatching of Special Envoy Kurusu to
America is Japan's independent action and America knows nothing of
it. This remark fully manifests the feeling harbored by Amorica to-
wards Japan. That is, this remark is a manifestation of America's
selfish attitude that Jupan can do as she pleases and America will
do likewise. In other words, America has no thought of the peace of
East Asia nor of the world, but only has the idea of preserving the
status quo and the ambition to dominate the world. Consequently.
should an unfortunate incident occur in the Far East, it is nothing
other than the responsibility of America. A minute ago, I mentioned
that America's only mbition is to dominate the world. Now let us
consider this remark in connection with the situation in East Asia.
The activities cf the ABCD encirclement, headed by America, have be-
come very active, of late. That is, America has taken over the
management of the Burma route for the purpose of checking Japan and
strengthening Chungking and has appointed Clareton (phonetic) as
Commander of the Air Force in the Philippines to strengthen the de-
fense of the Philippines. Again, a foreign dispatch of the 4th re-
ported that the American Government has ordered the evacuation of
American women and children from Guam and Wake Islands and President
Roosevelt on the 7th made public his intention to evacuate American
Marines from Poking and Shanghai. Furthermore, on the 4th, Secre-
tary of War Stimson nade a suggestion to the House Military Affairs
Committeo to cnact a law entrusting with the President the power to
proclaim martial law in Hawaii. In addition to this, America, in-
stigating the Panama Government, has made the said government take
action to exclude Japanese from Panama. Then, toc, on the 2nd, she
issued an order prohibiting the loading of mail on Japanese ships to
Japan. No matter what explanations America may make, these facts
are nothing other than the manifest tion of her hostile attitude to-
ward Japan. Such being the case, our country too is making every
preparation to cope with the situation and is watching America's
movements. When these facts are taken into consideration, it can be
seen that the situation in East Asia is being ....... by the nations
of the ABCD encirclement. This is the reason why Pacific problems
have become critical.
However, amidst this critical situation, Special Envoy
Yoshizawa and his party have landed in French Indo-China. This was
on the 4th of this month. At a time when Chungking is doing her
-5

=== Page 169 === (confidence 0.99)
reply to the actions of Stalin and President Roosevelt, Hitler made
a speech on the 8th and refuted the claimed successes of the Russian
Army, besides manifesting Germany's firm determination by stating
that Germany will welcome the landing of British and American
troops on Continental Europe.
Before closing, I would like to mention some more facts in re-
gard to America. That is, the Senate on the 7th adopted the neu-
trality law revision bill. As a result of this, American merchant
ships will be armed and will be allowed to sail through the war
zones. This fact literally indicates that America is now actually
taking part in the war, but the fact that such a thing is actually
being done by America, who is advocating peace by mouth, is very
inconsistent. In reply to this, Chancellor Hitler stated that
German submarines will carry out actions of self-defense and if
they should be fired upon by American ships, they will reply with
torpedoes, thus making clear Germany's attitude. Besides this,
America has made public her intention to establish military bases
in Iceland. This policy of America, lacking in righteousness, is
bringing unhappiness to tho people on the Atlantic and the Pacific.
In addition to this, Britain's movements in East Asia should also
be watched. Hence, Japan is watching the movements of hostile
nations with a firm determination and preparation.

Some time ago the Army and Navy established a policy of reward-
ing anyone who makes some sort of discovery or invention of military
value. The first receivers of these rewards were honored at the War
Ministry on the 14th. In all, 14 were honored and their inventions or
discoveries are already being used.
The propaganda motion pictures to be shown in foreign countries,

Provisions of formal treaty of alliance with Thailand:
(1) Japan and Thailand make clear their mutual final in-
dependence.
(2) If either party becomes involved in hostilities with
a third power, the other perty will give all aid,
politically, militarily and econcmically.
With regard to the aid in Paragraph 2, the responsible
officials of Thailand and Japan will cooperate.
(4) In case of Thailand and Japan making war together,
they will not nake seperate peace.
Comes into effect on the 2lst, to last ten years.
Words of Foreign Minister Togo in regard to Thailand-Japan
treaty of alliance: Japan and Thailand are both countries of
oriental races and tradition and have had a long period of friend-
ship and intercourse. It was believed that if this friendship
and intercourse vwere developed it would be mutually edvantageous.

News of the convocation of the 78th session of the

Electrical transcription of Premier Tojo's message to
the Diet put on. Opened his speech by urging the support of the
nation toward the government and stated that he had already expressed
the determination of the government to the previous session of the
Diet. Claimed that Japan had made every effort to maintain peace in
spite of the arrogant,atti tude of America. Explained the demands made

tite pact. Clsimed that if Japan were to comply with thesc three

tence also would be doubtful. Hence, he claimed Japan, though loving
pesce, had to resort to war, to defend her domain and existence.
Boasted of the successes achieved b: Japan's armed forces sincc the
beginning of the war, claiming that the main strongth of the British
fleet and the greater part of Anerica's navy had been destroyed.
Asserted that England and America are now on the road to destruction.
Stated that these successes werc attributable to the august virtue of
the Emperor and that the fate of the nation depends upon the outcome
of this war. Urged the solidarity of the nation to overcome the
present crisis and mentioned the untarnished tradition of Japan.
Claimed that England and America's ambition is to dominate the world.
Emphasized that Japan must accomplish her aim nd defeat th se two
brother enemies and that a footing capable of overcoming every diffi-
culty must be established, for the war is still in the future. He
urged the nation to have the firm confidence of ultimate victory, but
not to become drunk by the brilliant victories and expressed his whole-
hearted appreciation toward the cooperation being shown by Manchou-
kuo and Nanking. Explained that Japan had been compelled to take up

this end.
"Extortionists of E st Asia, Britain and merica will soon
be removed from that region," it was stated by the Foreign Minister,
he continued to say that Japan is obtaining groat victories toward
this imperative acconplishnent. (Carries on in the samo vein as
previous speaker, numerating the strong allies of Jepan ana tells
of the great cooperation and material aid reccived fron sac.)
The Empire of J pan has been founded to bring cll the races to their
proper respective places, no mater how long tho w.r may last. There
is no doubt that this will be achieved, he concluded.
Hongkong: The conquering General ..... ...... returned to
Canton and made the following statement to the effect that British
forces are no match for the Japanese. The British clained that the
lack of water was the reason for capitulation, but the truth is that
attacking Japanese forces did everything possible to preserve the
water supply.
Pictures of the Pearl Harbor attack are being shown at the
theaters in Japan. Pictures show beginning of trip and "shots"
of planes awaiting thir opportunity on the decks of air craft car-
ricrs. Shows the sinking and burning of United States captal ships
in Pearl Harbor leaving no doubt in the minds of the Japa ese that
the American fleet no longer exists as a threat.
Shanghai: Bangkok reports Chine se soldiers along the border
of Thailand for their alleged defense of Burma. Burnese cre robil-
ized, Indians are also being used, but still the number is srall.
The Burnese have been forced to arms by the British. This concludes
the news.
East Asia's New Year's creeti ng: (Reiterates Japan's reasons
and object for being at war. Repeats the brilliant accomplishments
of the armed forces. Absolutely nothing new. Claims again that
Britain and United States have been exploiting the peoples of East
-2-

Asia, but neglects to give examples. Describes sacrifices of

"have" nations i.e. U.S and Britain, and claims that they would

"have nots"; the situation was carried too far, forcing Japan to
to rise and champion the "have nots". Japan could no longer be
satisfied with the crumbs from the overflowing tables of the Anglo
Saxons (?). The old year of 194l has passed and with it has passed
the old system of Anglo Anerican domination. 1943 (?) will bring
a new hope, a new light for the progress of mankind no longer to
be subserviant to other powers. Japan the savior of the world,
etc., etc. The whole world is undergoing a complete transformation.
