mafore
Your 4069, Ju1y 22; 9 p.m.
The featuring by Japanese broadcasts of German military
successes in Russia does not of itself appear to us to be of
any special significance as it would seem to us that if the
Japanese Government should feel it necessary to do more at this
time by preparing public opinion for an attack on Russia such
preparations would take the form of a more direct appeal to
Japanese self-interest, as, for example, by emphasizing a
menace to Japanese security arising from the possibility of
Russian territory being made a base for military operations by
the United Nations against Japan.
/LI
We concur in the view that the Japanese would be likely
to attack Vladivostok only when convinced that the opportunity
C
1s a favorable one, but we believe that in their calculations
the measure of German suocess against Russia would be only one
factor in a situation involving consideration of their grand
strategy as a whole and of the effeot of such an attack upon
the course of operations elsewhere in the Pacific area.

PS/MEL
I have the honor to refer to the/Department's tele-
gram no./666, July 24, 1942, 9 p.m., concerning the de-
sire of the Board of Economic Warfare to obtain from
Chinese Government monitoring services items relating to
economic questions in Japanese radio broadcasts. As
stated in the Embassy's telegram no. 899 of August 1, 2
p.m., the Embassy has made arrangements with the Ministry
of Information to obtain copies of its daily confidential
reports of all Japanese radio broadcasts covered by its
monitoring service. The Embassy has also received
copies of the reports on Japanese radio broadcasts made
by the monitoring service of the CENTRAL NEWS AGENCY, the
official government news agency, and copies of all such
reports prepared by the Department of Information of the
Ministry of Foreign Affairs. The substance of the re-
ports received by the Embassy has been transmitted to
the Department for the Board of Economic Warfare in the
Embassy's telegrams no. 937 of August 13, 2 p.m., no.
960 of August 21, 10 a.n. and no. 1022 of September 9,
10 a.m.
As explained in the Embassy's telegram no. 899 of
August 1, 2 p.m., the number of economic items contained
in these reports is not sufficient to warrant a daily
telegraphic report to the Department and the Embassy, in
view of the absence of instructions from the Department
to the contrary, has telegraphed such reports only when
the

=== Page 306 === (confidence 0.99)
- 2 -
the material has been of sufficient quantity to warrant
so doing. The Embassy also suggested in its telegram
no. 937 of August 13, 2 p.m., that it be informed whether
the Board of Economic Warfare desired further reports of
that nature since the items reported were DOMEI despatches
which have possibly been received by American monitoring
services. No further instructions having been received
from the Department in this regard, the Embassy has con-
tinued to forward by telegraph such material as it has
received.
Transmitted herewith for the Board of Economic War-
fare is a translation of the economic material from Jap-
anese broadcasts which has been obtained from the three
above-named Chinese monitoring services and which has
formed the basis of the Embassy's telegraphic reports,
mentioned in the first paragraph of this despatch.
Respectfully yours,

=== Page 307 === (confidence 0.99)
No. 1 to Despatch No. 610 dated September 10, 1942
from American Embassy at Chungking
Translation of Economic Items in Japanese Broadcasts Obtained
from Chinese Government Monitoring Services.)
Tokyo, July 29. Direct wireless communication between Nanking and
Tokyo will begin on August 1, bolstering existing Shanghai-routed
telegraphic service between the two capitals. New service will
cut rates by 50% from rates charged for messages routed via Shang-
hai as at present.
Tokyo, July 30. Direct wireless communication between Japan and
the Philippines will be resumed tomorrow. Rate 1.80 yen for first
5 words and 65 sen for the next five in Japanese Kana.
Shanghai, August 6. Wireless communication service between Shang-
hai and Malaya and between Sumatra and Java were inaugurated today
through the facilities of the Central China Communications Company.
Direct connection with Manila is expected to commence within a few
days.
Shanghai, July 31. It has been officially announced that the
Central Bank of China and the Bank of Communications will be
permitted to reopen early in September under the supervision of
the Central Reserve Bank of China. Announcement was made by a
mixed committee of Chinese and Japanese banking experts. Reported
capitalization: Central Bank of China 20,000,000 yuan and Bank of
Communications 10,O0o,00o yuan. Previous shareholders who have no
connection with enemy or hostile elements will be given new shares
at ratio of 2 to 1. For the time being these two banks will operate
only in Shanghai, branches to be opened in central China at a later
date. Business to be conducted on the following principles: 1 - no
bonds will be issued; 2 - control of two banks will be invested in
Central Reserve Bank of China; and 3 - chief emphasis will be placed
on agricultural finance and business in the interior.
Hankow, July 31. Branch office of Central Reserva Bank of China
opened in Hankow. Fapi notes to be prohibited after two week period
for circulation of Central Reserve Bank notes. Fapi notes will be
illegal after August 10 in Wuhan area.
Hankow, Aug. 4. The branch office of the Central Reserve Bank of
China opened here today. Absorption of fapi in Wuhan area is
expected to begin Aug. 1o. The Central Reserve Bank expects to
start offices also in Kiukiang, Nanchang, Ichang, Shasi and Yuehchow
for speeding up the collection of fapi from the Yangtze area.
Sendai, Aug. 2. Minister of Agriculture and Forestry Hiroya Ino
predicted bumper crop of rice in Japan proper this year. Even in
event of drought in Korea, ample rice supplies assured by importations
from French Indo-China, Thailand and Burma. Japanese Government
taking comprehens ive steps to step up production of livestock and
agricultural products. Ample supply of sugar, as more than a million
tons of sugar obtainable from Philippines and Java. Shortage of crude
oil at one time threatened curtail operation of fishing boats, but
now more active than in pre-war days.
Shonan (Singapore), Aug. 3. Shonan City (Singapore) must be re-
constructed as a nerve center of southern regions under Japanese
control, Mayor Shigeo Odata said today. This city should become
not only a permanent base to link Japan with south for trade purpose,
but it also should become a self-sufficient industrial center. For
this

=== Page 308 === (confidence 0.99)
-2-
this reason, Odata said he was opposed to sending of all raw materials
produced in southern regions to Japan because Japan should not become
a second England full of factories. Singapore relied for existence
solely on materials brought by ships and it still depended on shipping
for materials. Although increased shipping will be made available in
future, Singapore will depend on present stocks until "Japan secures
an absolutely invincible position".
Tokyo, Aug. 6. Rapid progress of construction work is making North
China a powerful supply base for Japan, declared Shichiro Miura,
Chief of the Technological Section of the China Affairs Board.
Commenting on the importance of opening Tangku port, Miura pointed
out that the port was situated close to Peking and Tientsin and that
the construction work was scheduled to be completed by 1944. He said
that completion of the port would allow coal from Tatung mines as
well as other vital ma terials to be shipped to Japan. Miura also
revealed a five-year plan for constructing irrigation canals in North
China at a cost of 22,000,O00 yen to make North China sufficient.
Origina lly (pre-war) 14,Oóo kilometers highways in North hina - many
destroyed by Chinese troops. Japanese have repaired all existing
highways and have constructed 3,60o kilometers of new highways.
Tokyo, Aug. 6. Financially, the YOMIURI SHIMBUN states, the following
changes have taken place in Shanghai since Japanese occupation:
firstly, British and American firms have been put under supervision
of Japanese military; secondly, former fapi notes have been retired
from circulation; thirdly, factories, which were temporarily adminis-
tered by the Japanese military, have been returned to their Chinese
owners. With the establishment of Shanghai Municipal Chamber of
Comme rce, all business in Shanghai has begun to resume normal opera-
tions and speedy progress in industrial Shanghai is expected when it
begins to trade with the southern areas.
Singapore, Aug. 4. In view of the shortage of grains in Malaya,
which formerly relied on imports from Thailand and Burma, the Malayan
military and civil authorities are making every effort to give
guidance and to encourage production with a view to establishing a
self-supporting system. The week from Aug. 3 to Aug. 9 has been
assigned for taking forcible steps to increase food production in
Malaya, Singapore and Sumatra. (1o:30 a.m.)
Kuching (?), Borneo, Aug. 4. The Yokohama Specie Bank is adjusting
and re-establishing financial institutions in Borneo. Early in
July, branches were opened at Sandakan, Kuching (?) and Miri. A
branch was opened at Jesselton on Aug. 1, and anot her one will be
opened in a few days at Sibu (?). The confidence of local people
in the financial institution is increasing. The authorities accept
old Borneo currency at the same value as military notes, but people
like the latter much better. The tendency is that the military
notes will gradually take the place of old Borneo currency.
Tokyo.
While ast Asia is at war, a total savings of 230
million yen is expected in the rear in Japan. Officers and men
fighting on the front also save every cent possible and remit what
they have saved to their home towns for deposit as "military savings"
to take part in the "saving for the country" campaign. According to
statistics compiled by the army authorities, the military savings
and military remittances during the China Incident of past five
years amounted to 290,180,000 yen; and those during the past five
yen, totalling 350,520,000 yen.

=== Page 309 === (confidence 0.99)

anghai, July 24. In order to strengthen the "Central Reserve Bank"
nd to prohibit speculation on the market, the appropriate
authorities are drafting a plan for a more strict control of the
native banks, numbering over 1,000,in Shanghai. According to this
plan, a native bank with capital of less than $3,oo0,00o shall be
ordered to close or to incorporate with anot her native bank. Those
which have a capital of more than $3,000,000 subscribed by a number
of shareholders and which have been operating successfully shall be
permitted to continue operation. This restriction is considered
necessary to prevent speculation. There have been reports that many
native banks are exchanging old fapi in order to make profit. Since
the rules forbidding speculation are enforced, the exchange business
suffered considerably and most native banks in fact have no business.
To close these native banks will result in advantages rather than dis-
advantages. When hostilities broke out in ast Asia, a number of
native banks were opened in Szechuen Road exclusively for speculation
of currencies. They enjoyed good profit in January and February.
The appropriate authorities have now decided to impose restriction on
speculation so as to cut down prices. These native banks, which
directly monopolize the market, will no doubt receive a severe
blow. - Domei
Manila, July 30. Beginning the end of February, the Ministry of War
and the Manila Branch of the Bank of Formosa have been aujusting the
seven "enemy" banks in P.I. The work has recently been completed.
On Aug. 1, they will start liquidating these banks. Finance Minister
Kobayashi issued a statement on March l0 as follows:
"The liquidation of the seven enemy banks in P.I. by the Manila
Branch of the Bank of Formosa will begin on Aug. 1 according to
the following principles:
(1) The capital of the banks concerned and the deposits of
commercial firms whose head offices are in enemy countries or in
P.I. and whose trustees are mostly of enemy nationalities shall be
reguis iti oned.
(2) The requisitioned funds or property shall be confiscated or
utilized with authority of the Ministry of War. The confiscated
portion shall be credited to the annual receipts of the budget.
(3) The debts, deducting the amount of funds requisitioned, shall
hereafter be paid by bonds which shall a lso be used for depos it
drawings in the future.
(4) The drawing of deposits shall hereafter be effected by bonds
because when the war broke out all availa ble cash had been taken
away by enemy banks.
(5) Drawing of deposits with enemy banks shall begin one month
later.
(6) Enemy banks subject to liguidation are:
American - National City Bank of N.Y. and Peoples Bank &
Trust Co.
British - Hongkong & Shanghai Banking Corporation and Chartered
Bank of Australia, India & China.
Dutch
- Netherlandsch Indische Handelsbank.
Chungking- China Banking Corporation.
Philippines National Bank.

commenced this month according to the General Principles promulgated
by the Governor as provisional measures pending the organization of a

Batavia, Aug. 7. With a view to adjus ting demand and supply of
important farm products, the Products Department of the Ministry
of War has very recently established a Food Production Adminis-
tration to perform the following duties: adjustment of prices of
important farm products considered by the authorities as neces-
sities, purchase of farm products, export to and storage at
places within the "co-prosperity circle", control of granaries,
etc. The head office is at Batavia and a branch office at
Sourabaya. Sub-branches will be establ ished at other places
where necessity arises. Director Amahi of the Products Depart-
ment has assumed office as concurrently Director of the Adminis-
tration.
Batavia, Aug. 7. Army transportation authorities have decided
to facilitate highway communication on the entire Java Island.
First of all, a bus service will be inaugurated in Batavia
Municipality. Similar service will be opened at Bandoeng,
Sourabaya and Samarang. By the middle of August, a bus system
will be completed on the Island.
Manila, Aug. 7. Of the total capital of 2 million pesos needed
for the control of necessities in P.I., the retail merchants
ought to pay 200,000 pesos. Since the Retailers Guild is
organized by many retail merchant s, it is difficult to force
them each to share the same amount. It has therefore been de-
cided to use the system of subscription. The Guild issues stock-
shares with a guaranteed interest of 60 per annum. All Filipinos,
members or outsiders, are free to subscribe. This decision was
announced on Aug. 6. A total of 30,o00 pesos worth of shares was
subscribed on the same day. The Guild, in so doing, aims at the
restoration of prosperity and in the meantime absorption of idle
capital as well as the control of prices.
Hongkong, Aug. 1l. Market uneasiness resulting from devaluation
of Hongkong cur rency has gradually been pacified and military
notes have been circulating successfully. Consequently, money
market in Hongkong is gradually becoming active. The Rice and
Sugar Bureau began to use military notes yesterday. The tax
bureau, telegraph office, post office, electricity works, tram
cars, and water plant have been using military notes for a long
time. These notes will be used also in the purchase and sale of
other daily necessities. Military notes will be accepted every-
whe re in Hongkong.
Batavia, Aug. 1l. Native boats which took refuge in Sumatra,
Borneo and Celebes when the Imperial forces attacked Java have on
different occasions returned to Java. Navigation on the sea has
thus become active again. The Ministries of War and Communications
of Java have decided to utilize these native boats. With the
various important harbors of Java as centers, separate organs have
been established for registration of junks. In the past transpor-
tation by sea relied almost entirely on 30-50 ton junks. At
present, these junks still carry salt from Madura and other places
to Jeva, Celebes and Borneo harbors. Other necessities such as
rice, hemp, etc. are also being transported by junks to Sourabaya,
Batavia and Cheribon. The importance of transportation between
of Communications of Java has made a plan for ship-building.
Materials for this purpose shall in principle be obtained looally.
Machinery shall be supplied by interior for the time being and
preparations are being made to manufacture them locally in order
to attain the goal of self-support. (8:o0 a.m.)

Mentsin, Aug. 12. Japanese army and Chinese authorities, with a
view to maintaining peace of new China, have decided again to
strengthen economic blockade of Tientsin and vicinity. The
garrison commander of Tient sin issued a proclamation today as follows:
1) Transportation of, and dealing in, goods other than those
designated are absolutely prohibited. Transportation via routes
other than those specified is absolutely forbidden.
2) Time for transportation of goods is:
May to September - 7:00 a.m. to 8:00 p.m.
October to April - 8:00 " to 6:00
(transportation outside of above hours absolutely forbidden)
3)

(a) arms, ammunition and powder
(b) iron and steel of all kinds
(c) gunpowder materials, medicine, gasoline, cereals, daily
necessities, and cotton
(a) Hides, skins, feathers and hemp
Tokyo, Aug. 12. According to the MIYAKO SHIMBUN correspondent in
Shonan City (Singapore), all 18 member corporations of the Shonan
Rubber Association have completed restoration and reconstruction
works and have resumed operations. Some quarters fear an over-
production, but authorities of the Malaya administration will not
restrict production because new uses have been recently discovered by
Japanese research men, the latest being that of converting rubber
into fuel for propelling vehicles by a simple chemical process. The
MIYAKO correspondent predicts that it will not be long before all
automobiles in Malaya will be running on this rubber-base fuel.
Manila, Aug. 13. The Ministries of War and Finance have instructed
the Bank of Taiwan to formulate a plan for the establishment of
"extension" or branch offices at places of economic development in
the Philippines in order to provide a financial system locally.
Preparations are being made for establishing "extension offices" at
Cebu (on Cebu Island), Bacotod (negros) and Baguio (Luzon). The
first two will be opened sometime this month.
Peking, Aug. 13. North China Liaison Office of Asia Development
Board called a conference today for discussing measures to counteract
rise in prices. Those attending the conference are Chief Liaison
Officer Shiozawa and other officials ∞ncerned as well as representa-
tives of economic circle. In his opening address, Shiozawa stated
that the harvest of farm products in North China, especially in its
southern part, is better than what has been expected; that more
wheat can be purchased than first estimated; and he has confidence
in the suc cess of carrying out the measures to check rise in pric es.
After exchanging views, the proposal of Tsuchima, Director of the
North China Development Project, for organizing a People's Committee
for Assisting in Checking Price Increase was passed. The conference
then adjourned.

Tokyo, Aug.l6-17. The South Seas Spinning Industry Association
called a meeting on Aug. 15. The hitherto Industrial and Commercial
Councils of 3 municipalities and 3 hsiens have been changed to 6
municipalities and 6 hsiens. Arata Kishimoto, Mayor of Tokyo, has
been elected President of the Council. Discussions regarding
operation this year are also held. Japanese exhibits to the Thailand
Exhibition in Memory of the Promulgation of Constitution and to the
First Japanese Exhibition at Saigon shall be handled in the same way
as last year. Decisions are also adopted to send a Mission for
Assisting Reconstruction of the South Seas and also a Mission for
Investigation of Imports and Exports of Light Industry Products.
(Mission for Assisting Reconstruction of the South Seas) a total of
18 members to be selected from among the members of the Industrial
and Commercial Council for 6 municipalities and 6 hsiens, of which
first group shall proceed to Indo-China, second group to Malaya,
Burma and Sumatra, and third group to P.I., Borneo and Java.
(Mission for Investigation of Import and Export of Light Industry
Products) a total of 15 members to be selected from owners of light
industries at various large cities in Japan. The objective of this
mission is to promote imports from, and export to, the South Seas of
light industry products. The first group shall proceed to Thailand
and Indo-China, second group to P.I., third group to Malaya and
Burma and fourth group to N.E.I.
Production and Cultural Exhibition to be held at large cities in
Malaya, Burma, N.E.I. and P.I. for introducing Japanese products
and culture so as to increase the understanding of the South Seas
people.

Tokyo, Aug. 20. Japanese who have resided in America for many
years and who have now returned to Japan are organizing an
"Asiatic Development Association" in which technical experts
will be gathered for the development of Great East Asia. An
inaugural ceremony was held on August 20. About 400 persons
attended. A manifesto will be issued in which stress will be
laid on the development of barren land abroad so as to
contribute to the establishment of the "East Asiatic Co-
prosperity Circle".
Manila, Aug. 22. The Minister of Production, accompanied by
the Minister of Agriculture and Commerce, inspected Palawan (?)
and Pampangas, the chief grains producing areas of the P.I. to
see how the project of increasing production is carried out.
In the farming districts, about 80 to 90% have already had
paddies planted while a small part is still planting paddy
because they obtained seeds comparatively late. Paddies planted
in June are 17 or 18 inches high. Insects that people are afraid of
have not yet appeared. So a good harvest is expected next fiscal
year. For self-support of rice for military use, the Palawan (?)
farms have completed adjustment and out of a total of 2,ooo acres,
Formosan rice aa will be transplanted in 4o0 acres.
Singapore, Aug. 20. To echo the plan of increasing production in
Burma, efforts are being made to investigate and to transplant
cotton in the vicinity of Palembang, umatra. It is said that the
district is geologically suitable for planting cotton and an increase
of cotton production is expected. (9:oo a.m.)
Certain base, Aug. 20. The Japanese have been successful in
utilizing rafts for shipping lumber. Now the famous Chinese junks
are being used by Japan and "Manchukuo" for transportation purposes.
These junks are safe and economical, and are able to make shipments
in large quantities. The Japanese and "Manchukuo" authorities have
recently used 20 larger size junks, each with a loading capacity of
less than 1o0 tons, for shipping gunny bags to Manchuria - these
bags are needed by Manchurians for packing broad beans. The junks
took only little more than ten days to reach Dairen from Hongkong.
They do not need fuels and will be helpful in sea transportation.
They will be used in large numbers by the authorities concerned
hereafter.
Tokyo, Aug. 22. In view of the urgency of reconstruction activities
in the South, the East Asia Agricultural Research Institute, of
which the Minister of Agricult ure is concurrently the Director,
has borrowed an office in the Bureau of Agriculture of the Ministry
of Agriculture and Forestry to start operation. The Institute is
endeavoring to adjust and make r esearch in the following:
(1) Research in rural livelihood and rural labor at places in the
"co-prosperity circle".
(2) Research in biological resources (sic) useful to agriculture.
(3) Chemical research in the ways and means of preventing famine
and of increasing production.
(4) Research in general agricultural questions.
The main topics for research and investigation are:
(1) Domestic investigations.
(2) Economic and population problems.
The findings of such investigetions will make one understand
the agricultural and geographical ∞nd itions in East Asia so that
a general agricultural plan may be formulated. With the guidance
successful.

Tokyo, Aug. 22. The Ministry of Commerce and Industry issued on
Aug. 24 a proclamation appointing members of preparatory committees
for the control of cotton piece goods, rayon, wool and hemp. They
are instructed to form separate committees. The number of members
should be: cotton piece goods 92, artificial silk l22, wool 38 and
hemp 22. These control committees will report to the Minister of
Commerce and Industry before October 10 and will request approval
for their formal establ ishment. The present Fibre Products
Distribution Association will be abolished before long, and a new
legal person (public body) will be organized to handle the
distribution of fibre products. The Fibre Products Control
Committee will be organized by the above-mentioned four control
committees and Distribution Association after the establishment of
the four committees. The cotton piece goods and rayon control
committees will eadh have 10 supervisory members and the wool and
hemp control committees each have 5.
Batavia, Aug. 24. The salt consumed by inhabitants of Java has been
imported from Madura Island. fter Japanese occupation, the supply
of salt in Java suddenly became insufficient to meet demand
therefor. In view of such state of affairs, the military and civil
authorities have exerted efforts to increase production and to
improve transportation of salt. Bureaus for monopolistic sale of
salt have recently been establ is hed at Batavia, Semarang and
Sourabaya. These bureaus have begun to distribute salt to various
localities. Their efforts are generally speaking successful.
BOGUS "CENTRAL BANK" OF "MANCHUKUO" TO BE REORGANIZED
The "Manchukuo" Government has of late been studying the way
of reorganizing the "Central Bank of Manchukuo" in order to suit
the needs of the new situation. Having discussed certain concrete
policies concerning the reorganization, a conference will be called
for making an official decision, so that the bank will after re-
organization be able to conform to the new financial system enforced
since the outbreak of hostilities in Great ast Asia.
Tokyo, "ug. 7. According to an Asahi correspondent in Nanking,
the Chinese "National Government" issued instructions yesterday
to Sungkiang, Tsingpu, Kiashan, Wuhsien and Wukiang prohibiting
the use of fapi. These places are in the Taihu Model District of
Nanking. The instruction will be put into effect on Aug. 15.
The same instructions have been carried out sometime ago at Nanking,
Shanghai, Hangchow, Kiashing, Qunshan, Soochow, Changchow, Kiangying,
Chinkiang and Wusih. - Domei.

American members. All anti-axis banks, includ ing Dutch and Belgian,
will be dismissed. Those left will be 6 Japanese, 2 French, 1
Italian and 1 German banks. - Domei.

Janila, Aug. 10. The military and civil authorities in P.I.
announced today the revised production plan which provides for the
change in the production of sugar and includes a five year plan for
cotton production as follows: (Change in production of sugar)
1. Only a certain number of sugar factories with
output sufficient for domestic consumption shall
be retained. All others shall be used for manufacture
of alcohol directly from potatoes (?) and factories of
enemy nationalities shall manufacture fuels in order
to protect interest of potato (?) producers.
2. Only a certain number of potato (?) farms capable of
supplying the ne eds for manufacture of sugar,
shall be retained. Part of the remaining farms
shall be devoted to production of raw materials for
making alcohol and the rest for planting cotton.
3. The above shall be enforced as part of the 5-year
plan - from 17th to 2lst year of Showa. All surplus
potato (?) farms shall be used to plant grains.
(General principles of increasing cotton production)
1. During the 5 years from 1942 to 1946, total area of
cotton fields sha11 be increased to 455,000 mow
producing 1,500,000 piculs of cotton. In 17th year
of Showa, however, the area is only 12,00o mow producing
only 37,000 piculs.
2. The Cotton Industrial Society of Japan shall be the
organ taking charge of increase of production and
shall be authorized to plant and purchase cotton
as well as to do other business. A Philippines
Cotton Purchase Association shall be organized
to guide and ∞ntrol cotton production. It shall
make close contacts with Japanese cotton producers.
3. Purchases of cotton shall be made by authorized
parties either in case or by signing contracts.
The prices quoted in various districts shall
take into consideration both the expens es of
production and the benefit of cot ton industry.
Kalgan, Aug. 31. The Mongolian Government, with a view to providing
guaranty for exchange funds, desired to conclude an agreement with
the Japanese Government. Minister of Economics Numano has been
holding negotiations with the Japanese Ministry of Finance and
other authorities concerned. An agreement was signed at Tokyo on
Aug. 28 proyiding for an increase to a reasonably large figure of
exchange. These funds will, according to a statement issued on Aug.
31 by the Ministry of Economics, be used for development of
important resources needed in time of war and for importing
necessities for the Mongolians. The statement added that the
Japanese Government has determined to help the Mongolian Government
in this regard.
Saigon, Aug. 26. The barter agreement for the present year based
end of August will exceed 70%.

DEPARTMENT OF STATE
DIVISION OF EUROPEAN AFFAIRS
MEMORANDUM
February 6, 1943
Mr. G. Chaput of the Board of
Economic Warfare called to ask me if it
would be possible to arrange for more
frequent material from the Soviet monitor-
ing of Japanese radio broadcasts which
are being received through the Embassy in
Mosoow. Mr. Chaput said that this material
had been intermittent and at rather long
intervals. He also asked that we endeavor
to obtain from the Soviet authorities a
list of the Japanese stations especially
home broadcasts which are heard in the
Soviet Union. He said this information was
desired in order to permit Federal Communi-
cations Commission to check against the
stations which they are able to monitor in
the United States since there may be some
Japanese stations which could be monitored
but of which we are unaware.
I promised that we would take up the
matter with the Embassy in Moscow.

1/
Chinese Government monitoring services, and to transmit
herewith for the Board of Economic Warfare a translation

PS/TL
This material has served as the basis for the two telegrams
under reference.
Due to the discontinuance of the monitoring service
reports formerly issued by the CENTRAL NEWS AGENCY, the
amount of material obtainable has been decreased. The
reports of the Information Department of the Ministry of
Foreign Affairs are received at irregular intervals and
contain material for several weeks preceding: some of the
items reported by telegraph do not, therefore, represent
current materiel.

The characteristic feature of Malaya fi ance is the expenses
for reconstruction occupying an importnt part in the budget -
such expenses are for increase of foou roanction, development of
resources, resumption of transportation, sanitary activities,
police, education, ete. he receipts are land tax which is the
most important, and susiness tax, utomooil tax. The monopolistic
sale of opium and wines and tihe beer tax, stmp tax, inheritance
tax are also s urces of income. In the near future, the tariff
system vill also be resumeu.
sumatra will follow the elmaple of Maluya. Before the war,
there were many taxes such as roac tax, company tax, income tax,
ostal tax, poll tax. djustment vill be maue in those Dutch
taxes.
In the past, the income of Malaye vas used chiefly for paying
covernment personnel. At present, it ill be used to defray
expenditures in reconstruction activities.
Military notes are successfully circulateu in Malaya. With
Yokohama Specie leading, there are three banks financing the
development of resources - the other two being Formosa and south
China Banks. Chinese banks were on pril 28 permitted to reopen.
uick financial adjustments have helped Malaya and Sumatra to
avoid the hih rise of prices and inflntion of currency. Although
the prices are now high, they are not so high from t e viewpoint
of local conditions. is soon as the rosources are developed to a
reasonable level, such a state of affairs will eutomatically
cease to occur.

Hsingking, Oct. 4 - Minister Hwang of the Ministry of
Industry and Agriculture stated on Oct. 4 that the present war
in certain respect was a war of food stuffs; that the "government"
has following the outbreak of war exerted efforts to devise means
of self-support and has taken positive measures to supply Japan;
that emergency measures have been taken to increase production of
crops of which beans, rice and wheat have achieved expected
results - especially beans for Japan; and that tne "government"
is doing everything possible to make rural districts prosperous.

SECTION TWO
November 29 from Chanchun in Chinese:
One. There was held in Tokyo on November 26 and 27
an economic conference for Eastern Asia attended by more
than five hundred delegates from Japan, Mongolia, Manchukuo,
and China. The conference took note of the fact that since
the outbreak of the war great progress in the economic
development of the aforementioned countries had occurred.
In the future it will. be essential for the countries repre-
sented at the conference to maintain close relationships.
Two. On November 21 conferences were held in a number
of provinces of Eastern Manchuria for the purpose of con-
sidering means whereby agricultural produce would be
attracted to the markets. Crops in Manchuria this year are
not poorer than last year's crops.
November 30 from Changchun in Chinese: As of December 1
the income tax on cafes, restaurants, and various amusement
interprises in Manchukuo is to be increased.
December 1

=== Page 373 === (confidence 0.99)
-2-
December 1 from Tokyo in Japanese:
One. The treaty of aid between Japan and Thailand
received final approval today from the Privy Council. Each
day the friendly union between Japan and Thailand grows
stronger. Already we have treaties of cultural and mutual
assistance with Thailand.
Two. From Batavia it is reported that as of December 1
regular postal service has been established between Java and
Sumatra.
Three. In Northern Japan the harvesting has been completed
and the peasants are paying off their debts. During their spare
time they must now work in nearby shops and factories like the
peasants in other districts.
November 29 from Changch in Chinese: The Government of
Manchukuo has organized special courses of training for mining
technicians in order to insure the development of the mining
industry.
November 29 from Peiping in Chinese: The population,
particularly the peasants, are urged by a representative of
the Central Industrial Administration of Northern China to
observe strict economy in their consumption of food.
December 1 from Tokyo in Japanese: A report has been
received from The Railroad Construction Commission at

=== Page 374 === (confidence 0.99)
-3-
afuto that on Sakhalin a railroad and motor highway have
been completed.
December 1 from Changchun in Chinese: During the decade
which Manchukuo has existed, it has become independent
of imports and is able at the present time to supply Japan
with many essential commodities.and materials. During the
past year there has been an increase in the production of
coal and iron and crops were excellent. Certain shortages
resulting from the war exist. However, distribution is
satisfactory because of the rationing system. Further in-
creases in the price level have been prevented by the adoption
of the price stabilization law last year. Inflation does not
exist. The amount prescribed in the plan for popular savings
has been exceeded by one and one-half bi(*). Since the out-
break of the Asiatic War more than ten million yuans have been
contributed to the Kwantung army fund. More than five million
Juans have been offered as gifts. The Japanese Naval Attache
has received for the Japanese navy fund during the same period
more than sixty-three million yuans.
SECTION THREE
December 5 from Tokyo in Japanese: Under Japanese
direction the development of resources in Java proceeds.
Four million hectares of land are under cultivation. The
annual rice crop averages more than four million tons. This
year

=== Page 375 === (confidence 0.98)
-4-
year it will be larger than it has been during past years.
The production of sugar amounts to 2,300,000 tons. Of this
amount 300,000 tons are required for local consumption while
the balance is available for export. The production of cinchona
bark amounts to eight hundred tons annually. Rubber production
is 2,500,000 tons. Tobacco production amounts to 25,000 tons.
In cooperation with the local population, the Japanese
authorities are taking steps to develop rapidly the rich
deposits of silver, manganese, sulphur, gold, and zinc.
Deposits of other minerals are also being rapidly developed.

=== Page 381 === (confidence 0.99)
TRANSLATION OF SPEECH MADE ON NOVEMBER 26, 1942,
BY FORMER JAPANESE ENVOY KURUSU, AS BROADCAST BY
JAPANESE RADIO.
Exactly a year ago today---November 26th---was
the day on which Secretary Hull personally handed a
document to the two Ambassadors Nomura and Kurusu
after a lengthy period of eight months of talks be-
tween Japan and America, and on which the negotiation
practically came to an end. That document gave no
consideration to our proposals but enumerated condi-
tions hardly bearable to the Empire. Arrogant America
presented its unilateral views, at the same time se-
cretly planning to start a Great Asia War and preparing
a challenge to us. Secretary Hull's threatening words
to Japan were practically an ultimatum and indeed the
first direct mention of an ultimatum of war. Thus,
November 26th can be taken as the date on which America
issued its ultimatum to this Empire.
On August 20th of last year (?), the former Am-
bassador to America Kurusu returned home on an exchange
ship. While attending a luncheon party sponsored by
TA CHENG YI CHAN HUI (Imperial Political Assistance
Association) at the Imperial Hotel, he broke a silence
of three months after his return by declaring to the
public the true state of affairs in connection with
the Japanese-American negotiations and by exposing
fully the tyranny of America. Besides explicitly
stating where the responsibility for the war should
belong, he vigorously emphasized in the speech that
the people must show their firm determination to fight
the war to the bitter end.
Here is the important substance of the speech:
It will soon be a year since His Majesty the Em-
peror proclaimed the decree of declaration of war on
December 8th in the 16th year of Tsau Ho. To recall:
For ten months after the spring of last year the Empire
continued to take the attitude of courtesy and for-
bearance with the sole hope of securing peace and mak-
ing progress in improving the relations between Japan
and America. In the end, however, she was forced to
rise up courageously and to face the war with America
and Britain. This the Imperial Government has made
very clear to the world in its declared statements.
Significant was the day of last year when Ambas-
sador Nomura and I received from Secretary Hull a
very important document which brought the final stage
of negotiations to a close and to the crucial point
of peace or war. Thus, that day to me is an unfor-
gettable day in my life, and at the same time a day
which the pe ople of our own country must specially bear
in mind. Later in about ten days the two countries,
Japan and America, fell right into the midst of the
cannon fire. The Imperial Army, however, has not found
a real enemy in the war yet. In only about half a year
since

=== Page 382 === (confidence 0.99)
- 2 -
since the beginning of the war the Imperial Army has
already wiped out the American and British forces in
the entire Southern area and has reaped a record
harvest unprecedented in the history of world wars.
At the time when we were interned in the enemy country,
very often we received detailed reports, and, besides
offering our profound gratitude to the grace of His
Majesty the Emperor, we felt more than ever thankful
for being Japanese ourselves.
The factors causing the outbreak of the war in
the Great East Asia may be found in past history. In
reviewing the world development, it is found that all
the events which have occurred since the 19th century
have largely been struggles for power and have formed
a record for Western power over Eastern ......." All
the peoples of East Asia have been brought under the
advances of the strong military forces and economic
power of the Western (European) countries. This Great
Asia War, a natural sequence in the world history, is
but a revolt of the peoples of the East Asia against
the unlawful encroachment by the vestern (European)
power. Realizing this situation Germany, Italy and
other Axis Powers have on one hand advocated the es-
tablishment for themselves of a new order in Europe,
and on the other decidedly acknowledged the reasons
for the establishment of a new order in East Asia.
That is exactly why our country has taken coordinated
steps with these nations, has denied the schemes of
Britain and America, and has resorted to war with the
latter attempting to undermine our objectives.
Next, when we observe the relations of America
with Japan, we find that the Far Eastern policy of
America in the early period took primarily the develop-
ment of trade and propagation of Christianity as its
objectives, that then there existed the Furitan cons-
cience of Colonial America, and that the relations of
the two countries were thus most friendly and cordial.
But after the Spanish-American Nar in 1898 when America
took the Philippines and entered into the sphere of the
East Asia together with other countries of the West
(Europe), the American policy with respect to East Asia
changed its nature. There was outwardly shown a sign
of Puritanical idealism, but there were also in the
heart intrigues of imperialism. As a result, there
was an incongruous contradiction, or in other words,
imperialistic designs were too often disguised under
the good name of idealism. That kind of inconsistent
trend was most manifest after the Russo-Japanese War.
When the Manchurian Incident took place, America was
anti-Japanese and pro-Chinese, and the American policy
towards Imperial Japan was more outrageous than ever.
Then the China Incident broke out and America came to
aid China and maintain her antagonistic policy toward
Japan, even resorting to the use of threats and finally
to open war with Japan. On another side, witnessing
the growing prosperity of Nazi Germany, America un-
ceasingly insisted upon the threat from dictatorships
and

=== Page 383 === (confidence 0.99)
- 3 -
and went on with her planning of the increase of her
own armament program. When the Second European War
suddenly broke out, America declared that her national
defence line had moved to Europe, and accordingly she
busily indulged in the work of rendering assistance
to England. At the same time, she adopted in East
Asia a strong stand against Japan and stood on the
first line of aid for the Chiang regime. She used all
kinds of tactics to prevent our righteous development
in the South, because she had made a wrong estimate
of Japan, thinking that Japan, having already spent
most of its energy for four years in dealing with the
China Incident, could easily be brought to surrender
by means of economic pressure. At first she used em-
bargo as a threat and later she abrogated the Japanese-
American commercial treaty. At that time, our country
finding herself in a changing world situation had to
stand at the crossroads and to exercise extreme caution
in deciding its important national policy. But under
the repeated American pressure, our country finally
came to a decision to conclude the Tripartite Treaty with
Germany and Italy, to which America shortly answered
with the embargo of scrap iron. Again, immediately
following the conclusion of a joint defence between
the Empire and French Indo-China in July of last year,
America answered with an order of freezing assets and
another of prohibiting oil export. These are indeed
not measures of economic pressure, but important steps
comparable to the opening of the war. In the meantime,
just a few days before the enforcement of the freezing
order, Fresident Roosevelt made a speech in which he
said: "This kind of cconomic pressure may, of course,
lead to war, but it is something that cannot be avoided."
This statement was purposely made with the intention
to show determination as well as to hint that economic
pressure meant decision for war. Now, after the out-
break of the war, Assistant Secretary of State Berle
on February 20th of this year made a speech from which
it might be learned very clearly that America at that
time had already made up its mind to open war with
Japan. Furthermore, America's intrigues toward Japan
did not stop there. Besides economic war......
(here broadcast broken)...... to threat our country.
The Imperial Government, in the interest of pre-
serving peace in the Pacific, continued to exercise
forbearance and consideration working very hard toward
reaching an accord with America at Washington since
the spring of last year. However, on the part of
America, the above-mentioned steps like embargo of
oil and freezing of assets were all taken during the
negotiations, and there was throughout no indication
of a spirit of mutual compromise but, on the contrary,
a complete disregard of the actualities of East Asia.
In spite of all these, the Imperial Government
even at the last minute still attempted to solve the
problem, desperately hoping to prevent the possible
spread of war to the Pacific. It was with this idea
that

=== Page 384 === (confidence 1.00)
that I was instructed to cross the Pacific. At first
America showed only an attitude of social courtesy
toward our warm interest in reaching an agreement.
But all the way through she insisted upon the same
conception and the same principle as her keynote, thus
making delay in negotiations inevitable. With a desire
to relieve the tension of the atmosphere, we recommended
a modus vivandi on November 2oth of last year after very
careful and practical consideration. The contents of
the proposal are exactly what has been published. The
principal idea behind it was to relieve the growing
tension of the situation caused by the freezing order
and other actions. But to this America answered with
a document listing demands, which was personally handed
to me on November 26th, and which contained contradic-
tions........ including complete evacuation of troops
from China and French Indo-China, establishment of a
system based on the multilateral non-aggression treaty
concluded at the Washington Conference, and rejection
of our proposals. Thereupon negotiations came to the
last serious crisis, and war broke out.
The American proposed document of November 26th
and other documents have now all been published. One
American critic who had read this document once sigh-
ingly said: "It is not difficult to understand why
Japan decided to open war after receiving this kind of
document. But it was indeed wrong for the American
Government to hand such 'war-conducive' document to
Japan without first making it public to the people of
this country". As to what kind of attitude America
took at that time, it can be readily seen from an
American document published after the war. In the re-
port made on January 24 after investigation on the
spot at Pearl Harbor by the Roberts Commission which
was created to investigate the truth of the Pearl Har-
bor Battle, there are passages about long, careful and
coordinated preparations made by the headquarters of
American army and navy at the time of delivery of the
document on November 26th. It is stated: "The Japanese-
American negotiations have already come to an end under
such a deadlock that they cannot be opened again."
Thereupon, the Chief of Army Staff and the Chief of
Naval Operation respectively issued secret orders to
army and navy commanders in Hawaii together with de-
tailed instructions about necessary preparations for
the outbreak of the war. This can be taken as evidence
without a shred of doubt that the American Government
definitely made up its mind for a war with Japan. That
is why this war came so suddenly and the responsibility
of the war should be borne by America and not Japan.
However, from the document issued by America herself
alone it is still difficult to see the whole truth.
From the report of the Roberts Commission there are
many facts contained therein to prove that before Novem-
ber 27th, in connection with the Japanese-American ne-
gotiations, there were preparations in America for a
war with Japan. What should be particularly noted is
that in certain parts of the introduction in the report
there

=== Page 385 === (confidence 0.99)
- 5 -
there is a recognition of the possibility of conflict
between the American Pacific policy and policies of
other countries, and, following that argument, a
realization by American army and navy that when these
policies intended to avoid conflict could not be
brought into reconciliation, war would be unavoidable.
On January 24, 194l, Secretary of War Stimson issued
a circular letter stating: "The critical danger in
the relations between Japan and America has become
serious. The problem of safety of the Pacific Fleet
at Pearl Harbor should be examined again. And on the
basis of observation of the moment it may not be en-
tirely beyond expectation that Japan may attack Pearl
Harbour by surprise". It is indeed unthinkable that
America should, a year before, suspect others without
any ground. In a word, America was proud because she
had forseen the inevitability of the war with Japan.
Thus she showed no sincerity in the negotiations and
at the same time took the measures of freezing assets
and the like. America had evidently blinded herself
in the belief that Japan, having been wearied in the
China Incident, actually had no strength to fight a
combined force of America and Britain. The informa-
tion I received then confirms that some Americans be-
lieved that, in order to put Japan into a deep mire,
a war on the Pacific should be fought without regret
and that it was only a question for study as to how
to declare that war. There is also reason to believe
that there were not a few Americans who advocated
solidarity in the country rather than going into the
war. The navy and army commanders at Hawaii, the
Chief of Naval Operation, the Chief of Army Operation,
and others all believed then that since the war be-
tween Japan and America would be unavoidable, America
should take action before the Japanese. America was,
however, very timid with regard to the opening of the
war.
Since Japan occupied the Philippines, the American
country became panic-stricken and pessimistic over the
prospect of the war. At the same time the American
people boastfully declared that the aims of the war
were to destroy the stupid plans of Japan, to over-
throw the Axis Powers, to support democracy, and to
bring into realization the Atlantic Charter drafted
at the conference of Roosevelt and Churchill.
In the spring of this year Roosevelt himself be-
came highly interested and excited over this war. On
one hand, he named the war as "the war for survival",
but, on the other, he was not satisfied with this,
for at the bottom of his heart he embraced not only
the idea of bringing Japan into her knees, but also
the great scheme of placing the entire world under the
protection of the American flag. What is most signi-
ficant is the speech of Under-Secretary of State Welles
made on May 30th of this year. The New York Times re-
corded in this speech something about.........emancipa-
tion of all peoples after the war and the following
points as the aims of the war:
First,

=== Page 386 === (confidence 0.99)
- 6 -
First, Prosecution of individuals, groups and
citizens who are responsible for the war.
Second, Establishment of a peace organization in
a considerable time after the war, and then disarming
of the aggressor nations.
Third, Institution of an international police....
as a perpetual organization for preservation of peace.
Fourth, Attention to the solution of economic and
social problems after the war, and establishment of an
international structure with America and her Allies as
the basic members for establishing final conditions for
peace.
Fifth, Responsibility of America to assume leader-
ship in the world order after the war.
Sixth, Continuance of the Fan-American structure.
On the first point about the responsibility of the
war......
.......at the beginning of March of this year,
Britain's Foreign Secretary Eden and the Chungking re-
gime all manufactured reports of maltreatment of pri-
soners of war at Hong Kong and made a great use of the
material for propaganda.......The idea is to aim at the
punishment of the people of our country. Disarming of
aggressor nations in the second point and international
police in the third point mean........to place... ...
under military pressure of America, Chungking, etc.
The fourth and fifth points mean the placing of the
post-war world order under the leadership of America
and Anglo-Saxons. The sixth point means the continuance
of the western hemisphere as the sphere of influence
for America. As to the so-called world peace based on
economic freedom and equality, the primary purpose back
of it must be read together with the above-mentioned
fourth and fifth points.
In a word, they are all designed to create an
American system upon which all other countries must
depend so that when the latter have any kind of de-
mand, economic pressure or other means can be used to
bring them into surrender. Thus it is not difficult
to imagine what the aims of the war are. They are
none less than attempts to make our country fall down
so deeply as to be deprived of a chance to become an
independent nation again. That is one of the reasons
why our country must be well prepared to go on with
the war and to fight to the bitter end.
Translated by: TTL
Checked by: PDS

The following telegram concerns Japanese broad-

December 8, 1942 from Tokyo in Italian: (a)
Increased production of coal and oil in Borneo is
reported by the Japanese command. Industrial diamonds,
now in short supply, will also be received by Japan.
(b) Shipyards at Hongkong are repared and are in
full operation. The first large transport has already
been launched and the second will follow shortly.
There are also under construction many smaller craft.
December 12 from Tokyo in Japanese: (a) the week
of December 13 will be devoted to the mobilization of
provisions for the netional defense fund. A campaign
will be conducted throughout Japan between January 1
and April for the conservation of foodstuffs. All
patriots are urged to support this campaign as a
contribution to national defense.
(b) From Seoul it 1s reported that the Governor
General of Korea convened a conference to consider
the

=== Page 392 === (confidence 0.99)
-2-
the question of food conservation.
(c) The former leader of the proletariat party,
Katokanzi, in a radio address appealed to peasants
for greater sacrifices, greater efforts, and greater
production so that the armed forces would not lack
the necessary foodstuffs.
December 12 from Tokyo in Italian:
A new railway has been constructed by the Japan-
ese across Central Sumatra to advence industrial
development. With growing industrialization additional
transportation facilities are needed badly.
December 13 from Tokyo in Italian: (a) Many
named places in occupied territories have been
changed by Japanese authorities. The city and province
of Batavia have been renamed Jokarta. (b) It is
reported from Peiping that the construction of a net-
work of important trunk highways joining Peiping
and several provincial cities is almost completed.
These roads will have great significance in the
economic development of China and the exploitation
of its agricultural resources.
(c) On December 1 the postal services between
Singapore and the southern provinces were resumed.
December 14 from Tokyo in German: An announce-
ment has been made by Prime Minister Tojo that The
Japanese

=== Page 393 === (confidence 0.99)
-3
Japanese petroleum position at the outbreak of the
war was much worse than at present. Although it was
supposed by the Japanese that all oil reserves in the
Dutch East Indies would be destroyed, actually they
fell almost entirely into the hands of the Japanese.
Sufficient oil production is now controlled by Japan.
to meet its own military needs and to supply its
European allies as well. The Japanese fleet is fully
supplied. Since it is not threatened by submarines
Japan can solve the transport problem; furthermore
a substantial number of warships and merchant vessels,
including tankers, have been built since the beginning
of the war. The Japanese fleet increases steadily and
circulates freely in all seas.
December 14 from Tokyo in Italian: Popular con-
tributions to the War Ministry for armaments and relief
of war victims since the beginning of the war have
exceeded 86,000,000 yen; to the Navy M1nistry 137,000,000
yen. Contributions to the defense fund since 1938
exceeded 42,000,000 yen.
December 14 from Tokyo in Japanese: At the
next session of Parliament a bill for the establish-
ment of a Government petroleum trading monopoly,
which has been considered by the Cabinet and approved
by

production of articles of primary necessity.

Lande Fighters--At the outset of his address Tojo, on tehalf of the 100,000,000
people of Japan, pledged renewed determination to fight to win through to
victory. Tojo paid his respects to the officers and men of the Imperial forces,
who day and night continue to fight furiously with doath-defying spirit.

are vigorously executing the task of expanding Japan's fighting strength, for
the spirit of the brave and loyal men who died on the field of battle, and his
sympathy to the wounded and sick.

their sinister ambitions to dominate the world.

Reference the underlying copy of a
Tokyo broadcast as monitored by the F.B.I.S.
This contains a statement of the Tokyo
Board of Information in regard to the
Convention of Amity and Cultural Collaboration
between Japan a nd Bulgaria signed at Tokyo
on February 11, 1943.
After the usual preamble, the Conven-
tion (1) reaffirms the relations of amity
and confidence between the two countries,
(2) provides for the mutual promotion,
"as far as possible", of cultural relations
between the two countries, and (3) states
that the Convention became effective from
the date of its signature.
It is obvious that this Convention can
not be implemented to any degree at the
present time and that it is merely a
political gesture.

PARAPHRASE OF TELEGRAM RECEIVED
FROM:
AMERICAN EMBASSY, MoscoW
TO:
Secretary of State, Washington
DATED:
March 8, 1943
NUMBER: 129
SECRET
In connection with this message, I refer to my telegram
immediately preceding dated March 8, 1943, no. 128.
Since the beginning of the year, a marked difference in
tone has been noted in the broadcast summaries from Japan
received by the Embassy. Gone is the note of confidence and
triumph which marked the December celebrations. A pessimistic
outlook concerning the prospects for the final outcome of the
Pacific war succeeds the former note of triumph and emphasis
is stressed on the difficulties which confront Japen and its
allies in many fields, particularly in raw materials, agricul-
tural deficiencies, labor reserves, and supplies. In order
that the Japanese goals may eventually be attained, the people
are constantly being exhorted to the acceptance of new sacri-
fices and to supreme efforts to increase production. Little
talk of victory is now heard in these broadcasts. There has
not been previously noted such a frank statement of difficulties
facing Japan as that contained in the broadcast from Tokyo
on January 18 in the German language.
STANDLEY
DCA:CBF:JTM
3/18/43

PARAPHRASE OF TELEGRAM RECEIVED
AMERICAN EMBASSY, MOSCOW
TO:
Secretary of State, Washington
DATED:
March 8, 1943
NUMBER:
126
STRICTLY CONFIDENTIAL
Please be advised that I have sent the following
telegram to Kuibyshev under date of February 27, 1943
as no. 107.
The following summary of Japanese broadcasts should
be of interest to the BEW.
December 24, 1942 in Chinese from Chang Chun:
1. In order to establish a State stud farm in
the Charer district for the purpose of improving the
breed and encouraging horse raising in Mongolia, it
is reported from Kalgan that the Mongolian Government
has appropriated 300,000 yuans.
2. Simada, Director of the Manchurian Company
for the production and sale of articles of primary
necessity, stated upon his return from the economic
conference at Tokyo that in order to drive British
and American influence from Eastern Asia, the people
of Manchuria must make every effort to increase
their production of all types of goods.
December 26, 1942 in English from Tokyo:
1. It has been announced by the Japanese
authorities in Manila that reconstruotion work in
districts

=== Page 424 === (confidence 0.99)
-2
districts in the southern half of Luzon which suf-
fered damage as the result of military operations
has been completed. It is expected that traffic
will be resumed shortly on the railways which have
been restored through the efforts of the local
population and the Japanese authorities. Measures
are being taken to place all railways in the Philip-
pines in operation by the end of March. Thanks to
the cooperation of the Philipinos the railway con-
struction work which was begun in September was
completed sooner than expected.
2. Nanking reports that the authorities there
intend to t ake steps designed to strengthen the
Corporation for the Development of Central China
which at the present time comprises thirteen
separate companies and controls ten others. Closer
cooperation between Japan and China will be estab-
lished by the contemplated measures.
December 28, 1942 in Italian from Tokyo:
The Japanese command has established bases
in the strategically important Gilbert Islands
and has constructed a series of complex defensive
and offensive fortifications.
December 28, 1942 in English from Tokyo:
The program for the administration of the
Philippines

=== Page 425 === (confidence 0.99)
-3-
Philippines during 1943 has been published in
Manila. The staff of administrative employees
will be expanded considerably. The Japanese
Association will be reorganized so that it can
assist the administration. All schools and uni-
versities will be reopened. All mines will be
restored and placed in operation. Cotton cultiva-
tion will be extended to the entire country.
Financial provisions for this program have already
been made.
December 29, 1942 1n Japanese from Tokyo:
1. Chang Chun reports that the main inspec-
tion bureau has announced that it is planned to
build in Manchukuo an extensive network of railways
and highways. Work for this project will be per-
formed by recruits selected chiefly from among
re-settlers.
2. It was announced today by the Ministry of
Finance that the prefectures of Ibaraki and Yemagucci
had reached 90% of theirquota in the savings campaign.
The prefecture of Hirosima and Akiyamahave attained
80% of their quota.
3. The supply of gas for household purposes
has been reduced by 10% according to an announcement
by the Japanese Ministry of Interior.
December

=== Page 426 === (confidence 0.99)
December 30, 1942 in Japanese from Tokyo:
1. Prime Minister Tojo while conduoting an
inspection tour of the defenee positions in the
prefecture of Ibaraki appealed to the youth to
exert their utmost efforts for the purpose of
increasing agricultural production.
2. The Japanese authorities in Manila have
lent their assistance in establishing new banks and
existing banks have increased the scope of their
operations. From the intermediate schools opened
after the Japanese occupation 117 persons were
graduated today. The graduates will be placed in
construction work and in industry.
3. The publication in Tokyo of a weekly illus-
trated paper devoted to photographs of military
activities on the various Asiatic fronts has been
inaugurated. It is intended very son the same paper
will be published in Chang Chun.
January 2, 1943 in English from Tokyo:
Peaceful activity is developing on the Island
of Timor. Kupeng is the most important center in
this part of the Pacific. Agriculture is being
expanded rapidly, particularly the growing of vege-
tables. Japanese has been introduced in Timor's
schools.
January 2, 1943 in English from Chang Chun:
The

=== Page 427 === (confidence 0.98)
-5
The program for the development of Manchukuo
in the second phase of its existence and the law
introducing labor service for young people entered
into effect on January 1.
January 5, 1943 in Chinese from Chang Chun:
A series of measures designed to aid in the
development of agriculture is being planned by the
Government of Manchukuo. It was decided at a
recent conference of provincial agricultural direc-
tors to combine district and provincial experimental
stations and to make plans for their activities.
Attention will be given during the present year to
the preparation of agricultural specialists.
January 6, 1943 in Japanese from Chang Chun:
During the past year the number of opium dealers
in Manchukuo has been reduced 65%. The police is
successfully fighting against the sale of opium.
January 6, 1943 in Japanese from Tokyo:
In view of the necessity of regulating rubber
consumption a commission of 35o members has been
formed in the Ministry of Commerce end Industry to
handle questions involving rubber purchases.
January 6, 1943 in Spanish from Manila:
1. The Bureau for enlarging cotton plantations
states that the Philippines will be able to export
considerable more cotton this year than last. This
years'

=== Page 428 === (confidence 0.99)
-6
year's crop is very good, exceeding last year's, and
the quality is excellent.
2. Sanitary Services in the Philippines are
conducting an active campaign against contagious
diseases. Special santary inspection squads have
been organized in various provinces to induce
cleanliness and combat disease.
January 6, 1943 in Chinese from Chang Chun:
According to reports from Tokyo Prime Minister
Tojo yesterday visited a zinc factory in the out-
skirts of Tokyo and called upon the workers of
apply still greater energy and increase the output
of zinc.
January 8, 1943 in Chinese from Chang Chun:
It has been announced by the Ministry of
National Welfare that 100 students have been
admitted to courses in which they will be trained
as to how the plague should be combated.

January 7 from Tokyo in English.
1. It has been agreed by German and Manchukuan representative
to negotiate a commercial and economic agreement.
2. From Shangha1 it is reported that the Chinese
Nationalist Army's Chief of Staff has announced that it is the
intention of the Government to reorganize its armed forces and
to create a new technically equipped army. The Chief of Staff
also enumerated the steps which his government has taken in the
field of reconstruction and war production including the repair
of established railway lines, the construction of new railways,
the construetion of new factories and homes and construction
of special equipment for these factories.
3. It was stated by members of a Jap military mission on
their return from a trip to Java that the enthusiasm with which
the local population was cooperating in the work of reconstruc-
tion impressed them. Operations have been resumed on an
important railway line which the retreating Hollandeers had
destroyed.
January 12

January 12 from Tokyo in German.
1. The problemsof inoreasing the produotivity of labor,
the acoumulation of food reserves and the growth of war
industry were discussed by 7o representatives of various firms
at a conference convened by the War Council of Nationalist China.
2. On January 1l a new trade agreement between French
Indio China and Japan was signed at Saigon.
3. Professor Tanimuki of the Japanese Micro-biological
University after six months of research has isolated the bacillus
through which malaria 1s transmitted in the southern regions
of the Pacific and has discussed an antidote wnich completely
cures the patient within two days.
January 12 from Tokyo in Japanese. It was stated by a
member of the Coal Control Committee thet the war requires an
increased production of all types of goods especially coal which
is needed for transport, for the fleet and in war industries.
In using coal the strictest economy should be exercised by rail-
way workers. Coal miners must strive still further to increase
their output.
January 12 in Spanish. In Manila there has been published
several municipal regulations regarding retail trade. After
an interuption of two weeks the sale of butter will be resumed
through retail shops. In order to prevent increases in price
the public is requested to report to specially organized police
unit

January 14 from Tokyo in English. The Jepanese Government
decided to turn over l04 additional enterprises to their Chinese
owners after Nationalist China had declared war against the
United States and Great Britain. The 104 enterprises formerly

Japanese.
January 14 from Tokyo in Italian.
1. Eight factories operated by Japanese personnel, among
which are a flour mill, two textile mills and two cement
factories, have been turned over to Nationalist China by Japan.
ing
Japan still directs 36 factories belong to China.
2. Postal services have been very limited in occupied
New Guinea, Burma, Malaya, Hong Kong and Sumatra. It has only
been possible to exchange postcards and periodicals with Japan.
Postal operations are approaching the pre war level and letters
and books may now be sent.
January 15 from Tokyo in English.
1. From Peiping it 1s reported that it has been stated
by a representative of the North China Development Company that
production of iron ore and coal there is rapidly expanding.
This year one billion four hundred million yen has been
appropriated for railway and utility construction and noticeable
progress

=== Page 441 === (confidence 0.99)
-4
rogress is being made in this. It is anticipated that there
will be an excellent cotton crop.
2. From Singapore it is reported that postal service
has been resumed between Manchukuo, Malaya, French Indo China,
China, Burma, Sumatra, Java, Japan and Thailand.
3. A national economic council to direct the economic
development of the country will be organized in Nationalist
China following the adoption of a law defening the functions
of administrative agencies for the duration of the war. A
Ministry of Public Security will be set up and a Ministry of
Foods to control agriculture and to fix prices on agricultural
products will be formed.
January 15 from Changchun in Chinese.
1. An agreement for the exchange of radio broadcasts
between Manchukuo and Germany was signed at Changchun today.
2. At the Ministry of National Welfare a conference was
held today to adopt measures for fighting the plague. At this
meeting there was submitted a report on the campaign against
the plague in 1942 and plans were laid for the current year.
January 18 from Tokyo in English. Assistance in the
development of a scientific research is being given by the
military authorities at Singapore. New research institutes
have been created and the activities of those already
operating have been extended. An attempt is being made by
research

=== Page 442 === (confidence 0.99)
-5
research institutes to discover new sources of strategic
materials, to expand the food industry and to develop
local industries. In the Philipp&nes, Malaya and Java similar
institutes are being established.
January 18 from Tokyo in Italian. A report has been
received from Singapore that the Japanese authorities in Malaya
in December began preparations for working the salt mines.
These preparations will be completed by March and the
population of this province will receive sufficient salt to
meet requirements. It is believed that there will be an
available surplus for industrial use.
January 18 from Tokyo in Japanese. During the radio
broadcast Isikava Pirosi who was representing the Ministry of
Trade and Industry urged the greatest economy in the use of
consumer goods. A government decree concerning certain
organizations for the sale of various commodities will go into
effect on January 19. This decree must be observed carefully.
In order to provide supplies to the population according to
plan we were obliged on February 1, 1942 to introduce a system
of card rationing. The rationing system has fully justified
itself. All possible measures are being taken by the govern-
ment to increase the production of basic consumer goods but the
population must cooperate and reconcile themselves to limited
supplies.
January 18

=== Page 443 === (confidence 0.99)
-6-
January 18 from Tokyo in German. Special broadcast to
Germany.
1. It is a well known fact that certain difficulties
with maritime transport are being experienced by Japan. For
instance, recently a traveller in the South Seas might observe
a large number of oiling vessels which are used to transport
raw materials from the countries in this area. Furthermore
for transport purposes rafts which drift with the current are
being used by the Japanese.
2. At the present time people in Tokyo are discussing
certain restrictions in the supply of commodities which were
recently introduced with a view to organizing distribution more
rationally during the war. There has been a reduction in the
rations of textile goods and cigarettes. Larger quantities of
uncleaned rice will be used. Of course the Japanese are
seriously effected by the rationalization but it is realized
by them that they must live less comfortably to attain
important military objectives.
January 22 from Chang Chun in Chinese. An important
condition for the realization of the economic development of
Manchukuo is the availability of an adequate reserve of man-
power. Labor service has been introduced by the government
for this reason. At the present time a considerable portion
of the rural population is now mobilized for industrial work
1n

=== Page 444 === (confidence 0.99)
-7
1 countries. There is a large population in Manchukuo
but it also has a large territory; for this reason our
agricultural situation will deteriorate still further. The
government has been considering means of improving agricultural
equipment and modernizing methods of cultivation in order to
release a portion of farm workers to industry and to take up
the slack in agriculture. Also the government has been making
every effort to recruit workers in North China and they have
reached an agreement with the authorities there on this question.
However, since the turn over is high and large sums of money
are sent out of the country, foreign workers are unsatisfactory.
In the areas of the South Pacific which the Jap forces occupy
there are large resources of raw materials available but these
materials cannot be transported. Accordingly, measures must
be taken by Manchukuo to produce more iron for Japan and it is
essential that the coal production be increased correspondingly.
Therefore, it has been decided by the government that during
their free time all peasants shall work in the minesalthough
they may return home when necessary to work the fields. The
custom of sitting at home should be abandoned by Manchurian
women and they should take their husbands' places in the fields
thereby freeing men for industrial work.
January 24 from Tokyo in Italian. A commercial agreement
between Shanghai and Manila has gone into effect. Paper and
other

=== Page 445 === (confidence 0.96)
-8-
other goods will be exchanged for Philipping goods such as
jute and sugar.
The foregoing should be brought to the attention of the
Board of Economic Warfare.

STRICTLY CONFIDENTIAL
Reference is made herewith to the Department's
wire to Moscow of February 10, 1943, no. 77.
In a note to the Foreign Office dated February 17,
this Embassy requested a list of the Japanese radio
stations, including home broadcasts, with wave lengths
and schedules which are audible in the Soviet Union.
Up to now no reply to the Embassy's note has been
received.

frequent reports, particularly when they are able to
overcome the shortage of personnel existing there.
This telegram has been repeated to Moscow for the
information of our officers.

SUBJECT:
Objectives, Character and Effect of
1-1055
Japanese Propaganda Directed to
